Trace the full lifecycle of a stock item from Sales Order through to final accounting: Sales Order, Delivery Note, and Sales Invoice. Given that a Delivery Note can be submitted well before the corresponding Sales Invoice, how does ERPNext ensure the cost of goods sold is booked exactly once rather than twice? Name the relevant functions, classes and files across all three doctypes.

final answer
**Short answer:** The Delivery Note (DN) books the cost of goods sold (COGS) once. The Sales Invoice (SI) books only income and receivables, unless `update_stock` is on. In that case it can't be linked to a DN. If the company uses the optional "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" (SDBNB) account, the SI reverses the DN's SDBNB booking into the real expense account. I read the code paths below but did not run any of it. ## 1. Sales Order → Delivery Note / Sales Invoice - **Mapping:** `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py` has `make_delivery_note` (line 232) and `make_sales_invoice` (line 425). They create the downstream document and set `against_sales_order`, `so_detail`, `sales_order` on the items. - **Stock effect:** The Sales Order (`sales_order.py`, class `SalesOrder`) posts no stock ledger or GL entries. It only reserves stock through `update_reserved_qty`, which updates `Bin.reserved_qty`. It also tracks progress through `update_status`. ## 2. Delivery Note: stock out and COGS - **Mapping to invoice:** `erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/mapper.py` has `make_sales_invoice` (line 62). It links the invoice rows through `delivery_note` and `dn_detail`. - **On submit** (`delivery_note.py`, class `DeliveryNote`): - `on_submit` calls `update_prevdoc_status`, which updates the Sales Order's `delivered_qty`. - It also calls `update_billing_status`. - It writes the Stock Ledger Entries through `update_stock_ledger`. - It then calls `make_gl_entries`. - **GL composition:** `DeliveryNote.get_gl_entries` calls `DeliveryNoteGLComposer.compose` (`stock/doctype/delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py`). That class extends `BaseStockGLComposer` (`stock/services/base_stock_gl_composer.py`), which posts the stock-in-hand credit and expense debit at the Stock Ledger valuation. The expense account comes from `get_voucher_details`. - **Choosing the expense account:** `DeliveryNote.validate_expense_account` sets `item.expense_account`. - If the company has `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` and a `stock_delivered_but_not_billed` account, each stock item's `expense_account` becomes the SDBNB account. - Otherwise the item gets the default COGS account. - Items with `against_sales_invoice` can't use the SDBNB account. ## 3. Sales Invoice: income and receivable, not stock - **Class and GL composer:** `SalesInvoice` is in `accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py`. `get_gl_entries` calls `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.compose` (`accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`). The composer posts customer, tax, and income entries through `make_customer_gl_entry`, `make_tax_gl_entries` and `make_item_gl_entries`. It also posts POS, write-off and rounding entries. - **Stock and COGS are gated:** The base class `get_gl_entries()` is called only `if cint(doc.update_stock) and perpetual inventory is enabled` (line 374). This is the base stock GL loop in `stock_controller.py`, and it is what posts COGS. So a normal invoice created from a DN, with `update_stock=0`, books no COGS. - **Double-booking guard:** `SalesInvoice.validate_delivery_note` throws "Stock cannot be updated against the following Delivery Notes" if `update_stock` is on and any item has a `delivery_note`. The invoice can't both move stock and be linked to a DN. `SalesInvoice.on_submit` and `on_cancel` only call `update_stock_ledger` when `update_stock == 1`. - **SDBNB reversal:** If SDBNB is enabled, `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries` runs. It returns early when `doc.update_stock` is set or perpetual inventory is off. - `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item` finds the DN item's `expense_account`. It then reads the DN's Stock Ledger Entry through `voucher_no` and `voucher_detail_no=dn_detail`, and computes `valuation_rate * item.stock_qty`. - `_is_sdbnb_reversal` confirms the DN account has account type "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" and differs from the invoice item's expense account. - `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries` credits the SDBNB account and debits the invoice item's real COGS account for that amount. - The DN therefore debits SDBNB, a temporary balance-sheet-style account. The SI later moves that amount into COGS. The invoice amount uses the DN's own valuation, so nothing is revalued. - **Only the invoiced quantity is moved:** The reversal amount uses `item.stock_qty` from the invoice row, so partial invoicing reverses only the billed part. ## 4. Keeping the documents in sync - **Billing status:** `SalesInvoice.update_billing_status_in_dn` calls `update_billed_amount_based_on_so` (`delivery_note/services/billing_status.py`) and DN's `update_billing_status` (also in `billing_status.py`). These update `billed_amt` and `per_billed` on the DN and its Sales Order. - **Returns:** `SalesInvoice.update_billing_status_in_dn` skips returns unless `update_billed_amount_in_delivery_note` is set. DN returns use `disable_sdbnb_in_sr` to put the return on the default expense account instead of SDBNB. ## Net effect - **Delivery Note:** stock leaves the warehouse, and the DN posts Dr COGS (or SDBNB) / Cr Inventory. - **Sales Invoice (`update_stock=0`):** Dr Receivable / Cr Income, plus Dr COGS / Cr SDBNB if SDBNB is enabled. It posts no second inventory credit. - **Sales Invoice (`update_stock=1`):** it posts the stock movement and COGS itself, and it can't reference a DN.

final answer
Here is the lifecycle, with the mechanism that stops COGS being booked twice. I read the code paths below, but I did not run any of it. ## 1. Sales Order - **Files:** `selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py` and `selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py`. - **Stock effect:** Submitting a Sales Order books no stock ledger entries and no accounting entries. It only reserves stock: the Bin's `reserved_qty` goes up, and optional Stock Reservation Entries can be created. - **Delivery Note:** `make_delivery_note` in the mapper creates it, and each Delivery Note Item carries `so_detail` and `against_sales_order`. - **Sales Invoice:** `make_sales_invoice` in `sales_order/mapper.py` creates it. `update_item` and `get_billed_qty` work out the remaining billable quantity by summing submitted Sales Invoice Items that carry the same `so_detail`. ## 2. Delivery Note - **Files:** `stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py` (class `DeliveryNote`), `delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py` (`DeliveryNoteGLComposer`) and `delivery_note/mapper.py`. - **`on_submit` order:** 1. `update_prevdoc_status` updates `delivered_qty` and `per_delivered` on the Sales Order. 2. `update_billing_status` updates `per_billed` on the Delivery Note. 3. `update_stock_reservation_entries` updates reservations. 4. `update_stock_ledger` creates the Stock Ledger Entries (SLEs) for the delivered stock, using the valuation rate from `stock/stock_ledger.py`. This ledger update works through `update_entries_after`, and `update_outgoing_rate_on_transaction` writes the outgoing rate back to the row. 5. `make_gl_entries` calls `get_gl_entries`, which delegates to `DeliveryNoteGLComposer.compose`. Under perpetual inventory it books Dr Expense account, Cr Inventory account. 6. `repost_future_sle_and_gle` reposts later entries if needed. - **`validate_expense_account`:** For stock items in a Delivery Note that is not billed against a Sales Invoice, if the company has `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` and a `stock_delivered_but_not_billed` account set, this method forces `item.expense_account` to that account. Sales returns can be excluded with `disable_sdbnb_in_sr`. - **Result of that:** The Delivery Note's debit side is a holding account of type "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" (SDBNB), not Cost of Goods Sold (COGS). The Delivery Note records that the stock left and what it cost. It is not yet final COGS. - **Without SDBNB:** Without SDBNB enabled, the Delivery Note books COGS directly. - **Guards:** - `validate_expense_account` raises an error if an item linked to a Sales Invoice (`against_sales_invoice`) uses the SDBNB account. - `check_next_docstatus` blocks cancelling a Delivery Note that has a submitted invoice. - **Billing link:** - `update_billing_status` and `delivery_note/services/billing_status.py` (`BillingStatusService`) keep `billed_amt` and `per_billed` current. - `delivery_note/mapper.py::make_sales_invoice` copies each Delivery Note Item name into the invoice row as `dn_detail`, and also sets `delivery_note`. ## 3. Sales Invoice - **Files:** `accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py` (class `SalesInvoice`) and `services/gl_composer.py` (`SalesInvoiceGLComposer`). - **Invoice created from a Delivery Note:** `update_stock` is 0, so the invoice does not touch the stock ledger. - **Guard:** `validate_delivery_note` raises "Stock cannot be updated against the following Delivery Notes" if `update_stock` is on and any item has a `delivery_note`. A Sales Invoice linked to a Delivery Note therefore cannot post stock a second time. - **Related checks:** - `validate_multiple_billing("Delivery Note", "dn_detail", "amount")` stops the same Delivery Note row being billed more than its amount. - `update_billing_status_for_zero_amount_refdoc` and the `dn_detail`-based billed-amount update in `SalesInvoice` write the billed amount back to the Delivery Note and Sales Order. - `validate_delivery_note` and the check around line 1025 require the linked Delivery Note to be submitted. - **Accounting:** `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.compose` builds the entries in this order: 1. `make_customer_gl_entry` 2. `make_tax_gl_entries` 3. `make_item_gl_entries` (income entries) 4. SDBNB reversal, when the company has SDBNB enabled 5. discount entries 6. POS, write-off and rounding entries - **Stock entries only when the invoice moved stock:** Inside `make_item_gl_entries`, the call `super(SalesInvoice, doc).get_gl_entries()` (the stock controller's COGS and inventory entries) runs only `if cint(doc.update_stock) and perpetual inventory is on`. An invoice created from a Delivery Note skips it. - **SDBNB reversal:** - `stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries` returns early if `doc.update_stock` is set or perpetual inventory is off. Otherwise, for each item it calls `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item`, then `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries`. - `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item` applies only to stock items that have `delivery_note` or `dn_detail`. It reads the Delivery Note Item's `expense_account`. - `_is_sdbnb_reversal` confirms that account is of type "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" and differs from the invoice item's `expense_account`. - The valuation amount is the Delivery Note's own SLE, `stock_value_difference / actual_qty`, multiplied by the invoice's `item.stock_qty`. Billing therefore uses the same cost the Delivery Note booked, and a partial invoice reverses only its own share. - `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries` then posts Cr SDBNB and Dr the item's real COGS account (`item.expense_account`). ## Why COGS is booked exactly once 1. **Stock movement, and the cost it books:** `update_stock_ledger` and the Delivery Note's GL entries run only on the Delivery Note when the invoice is linked to one. The invoice's `update_stock` is forced to 0, and `validate_delivery_note` prevents it from being switched on. 2. **COGS timing:** With SDBNB, the Delivery Note's cost sits in the SDBNB account. The invoice then moves that same amount from SDBNB to COGS, with an offsetting Cr SDBNB. COGS is recognised once, when the invoice is submitted. Without SDBNB, the Delivery Note books COGS itself and the invoice books no cost entry. 3. **No double counting if you invoice directly:** An invoice with `update_stock=1` and no Delivery Note books Dr COGS, Cr Inventory itself, via the stock controller path. In that case no Delivery Note exists for the same goods. 4. **Reposting:** `repost_item_valuation.py::_update_post_delivery_billed_vouchers` is called from `repost_gl_entries`. It finds submitted invoices whose `dn_detail` or `delivery_note` points at a reposted Delivery Note and reposts them too. This keeps the SDBNB reversal matched to the revised Delivery Note valuation. The code that decides this lives in `stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py`, `stock/doctype/delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py`, `accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py` and `accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`. The shared stock-ledger and GL plumbing is in `controllers/stock_controller.py` (`make_gl_entries`, `get_gl_entries`) and `stock/stock_ledger.py`. I did not open `delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py` in detail. I only confirmed it exists and has `enforce_pl_expense_account = False`. The exact Delivery Note GL entries therefore come from the method names and the inherited stock-controller behaviour.

final answer
**Short answer:** ERPNext books cost of goods sold (COGS) once, because exactly one document posts the stock movement and one entry carries the cost. A Delivery Note (DN) posts the stock ledger and the stock-to-expense GL entries. A Sales Invoice (SI) created from that DN has `update_stock = 0`. It posts no stock ledger entries and no stock/COGS entries. If the company has the "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" (SDBNB) feature on, the SI only moves the cost from the SDBNB account into COGS. I read the code for most of this. I did not run it, and I did not open `BaseStockGLComposer`, the status-updater internals, or `sales_order.py`. ## 1. Sales Order (`erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py`) - I did not open this file. I only saw its child doctype, Sales Order Item, referenced from the other two. - The Sales Order posts no stock ledger and no GL entries. - It only tracks progress through the `delivered_qty` and `billed_amt` fields on its items, and `per_delivered` and `per_billed` on the order. - `make_delivery_note` and `make_sales_invoice` create the downstream documents. I inferred this from the usual layout and did not verify it in the file. - Each downstream item carries `so_detail`, the link back to the Sales Order Item. ## 2. Delivery Note (`erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py`, class `DeliveryNote`) - **Status updates:** `__init__` builds the `status_updater` list. `on_submit` calls `update_prevdoc_status()`, which writes `delivered_qty` and `per_delivered` back to Sales Order Item. The same list updates Sales Invoice Item `delivered_qty` through `si_detail`, and Pick List Item. The `second_source_dt` entry also counts Sales Invoice Items with `update_stock = 1` toward delivery. - **Billing status:** `update_billing_status()` delegates to `BillingStatusService` in `services/billing_status.py`. - **Stock ledger:** `on_submit` calls `update_stock_ledger()`. The DN is the doc that posts the Stock Ledger Entry (SLE), where `voucher_type` is Delivery Note and `voucher_detail_no` is the DN item name. - **GL entries:** - `get_gl_entries()` uses `DeliveryNoteGLComposer`, in `services/gl_composer.py`. It extends `BaseStockGLComposer` and only sets `enforce_pl_expense_account = False`. - The base composer, called through `StockController.make_gl_entries` in `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`, builds the usual entries from the SLE: Dr expense account, Cr inventory (stock) account. - **Expense account:** `validate_expense_account()` chooses the account for each stock item. - If the company has `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` on, the item's `expense_account` becomes the company's `stock_delivered_but_not_billed` account. - Delivery then books Dr SDBNB (an asset account), Cr Stock, so no P&L cost is recognised yet. - Items with `against_sales_invoice` are blocked from using the SDBNB account. - If the feature is off, the DN books COGS directly. - **Return handling:** returns use `disable_sdbnb_in_sr` to fall back to the default expense account. ## 3. Sales Invoice (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py`, class `SalesInvoice`) - **No second stock posting:** - `on_submit` calls `update_stock_ledger()` only `if self.update_stock == 1`. The same check guards the stock reservation call and the cancel path. - An SI made from a DN keeps `update_stock = 0`, so it creates no SLE. - `validate_delivery_note` and the `dn_required` check make sure the item is linked to a DN when delivery notes are required. - `SalesInvoice.get_gl_entries()` uses `SalesInvoiceGLComposer` (`services/gl_composer.py`). When `update_stock` is 0, `make_item_gl_entries` posts only income, receivable and tax entries. It adds no stock/COGS entries, because that step runs only when `cint(doc.update_stock)` and perpetual inventory are both on. - **Moving the cost out of SDBNB:** - `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries()` returns early when `update_stock` is set or perpetual inventory is off. - For each item it calls `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item()`. That method needs `item.delivery_note` or `item.dn_detail`, and the item must be a stock item. - It then reads the DN item's `expense_account`, and `_is_sdbnb_reversal()` checks that account is of type "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" and differs from the SI item's own `expense_account`. - Next it looks up the DN's SLE by `voucher_no`, `voucher_detail_no = dn_detail` and `is_cancelled = 0`. It takes the valuation rate as `stock_value_difference / actual_qty`, and the amount as that rate times `item.stock_qty`. - `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries()` posts Cr SDBNB (the DN's expense account) and Dr the SI item's `expense_account` (COGS). - The amount is the DN's actual valuation rate times the invoiced quantity. A partial invoice therefore moves only the invoiced share, and the SI doesn't recompute cost from current stock. - **Linkage fields:** `dn_detail` and `delivery_note` on Sales Invoice Item, and `si_detail` on Delivery Note Item. ## How COGS is booked exactly once 1. The SLE, and therefore the stock valuation, is created only by the DN (or by an SI with `update_stock = 1`, never both). The SI's stock check uses `update_stock`, and the DN-linked quantity is tracked through `delivered_qty`. 2. With SDBNB on, the DN's debit goes to the SDBNB asset account. The SI later reverses exactly that amount from SDBNB into COGS. COGS is recognised once, at invoicing, and SDBNB nets to zero. 3. With SDBNB off, the DN debits COGS directly. The SI posts no stock/COGS entries, because `update_stock = 0` and `stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries` returns nothing. 4. `SalesInvoiceGLComposer` takes the amount from the DN's own SLE, so it matches exactly what was booked at delivery. The DN `on_submit` also calls `update_prevdoc_status()` and `update_billing_status()`, and the SI has its own status service in `services/status.py`. That is how the Sales Order's delivered and billed percentages stay in step.

final answer
ERPNext books COGS once by making the document that moved the stock the only one that posts a stock entry. For a delivered-then-billed item, that document is the Delivery Note. I traced this by reading the code. I did not run any of it. ## Lifecycle **1. Sales Order** - Files: `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py` and `mapper.py`. The `SalesOrder` class extends `SellingController`. - A Sales Order has no stock ledger entry (SLE) or GL entry. At most it reserves stock (`update_stock_reservation_entries`) and updates the Bin's reserved quantity. - The UI buttons in `sales_order.js` (`make_delivery_note`, `make_sales_invoice`) call the mapper functions `make_delivery_note` and `make_sales_invoice` in `sales_order/mapper.py`. - Those mappers copy the order rows into the new document. They set `so_detail` and `against_sales_order` on Delivery Note Items, and `sales_order` and `so_detail` on Sales Invoice Items. - In `make_sales_invoice`, `update_item` sets the quantity to `qty − billed qty` (or `qty − returned_qty`). It sets the amount to `amount − billed_amt`. **2. Delivery Note** - File: `erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py`. The `DeliveryNote` class extends `SellingController`, then `StockController`. - `on_submit` runs these steps in order: - `update_prevdoc_status()` updates `delivered_qty` and `per_delivered` on the Sales Order. - `update_billing_status()` updates billing status. - `update_stock_ledger()` posts the stock-out SLE at valuation rate. - `make_gl_entries()` calls `get_gl_entries`, which delegates to `DeliveryNoteGLComposer.compose` (`stock/doctype/delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py`). This is the standard `StockController` stock GL: credit Stock In Hand, debit the item's `expense_account`. - `repost_future_sle_and_gle()` reposts later stock and GL entries. - `validate_expense_account` sets each Delivery Note Item's `expense_account` to the company's Stock Delivered But Not Billed (SDBNB) account when `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` is on. In that case the delivery debits SDBNB rather than the COGS account, so COGS is not booked yet. - Items that already come from a Sales Invoice (`against_sales_invoice`) are not allowed to use SDBNB. - The Delivery Note tracks billing in `Delivery Note Item.billed_amt` and `per_billed`: - `update_billing_status` is in `delivery_note/services/billing_status.py`. - `update_billed_amount_based_on_so` is in the same file. - `update_billing_percentage` is in `StockController`. **3. Sales Invoice** - Files: `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py` and `services/gl_composer.py`. The `SalesInvoice` class extends `SellingController`. - There are two ways to create the invoice: - `delivery_note/mapper.py::make_sales_invoice` sets `dn_detail` and `delivery_note` on each row. It uses `get_invoiced_qty_map` and `get_returned_qty_map` to compute the pending quantity. It throws "All these items have already been invoiced/returned" if nothing is left. - `sales_order/mapper.py::make_sales_invoice` sets `so_detail` but not `dn_detail`. - `SalesInvoice.on_submit` calls these, in this order: - `update_prevdoc_status()` updates the Sales Order's billed amount and `per_billed`. - `update_billing_status_in_dn()` updates the Delivery Note. - `update_stock_ledger()` runs only `if update_stock == 1`. - `make_gl_entries()` posts the accounting entries. - `update_billing_status_in_dn` sums the `amount` of submitted Sales Invoice Items with the same `dn_detail`. It writes the total to `Delivery Note Item.billed_amt`, falling back to `update_billed_amount_based_on_so` for `so_detail` rows. It then calls `DeliveryNote.update_billing_percentage`. - `BillingValidationService.validate_multiple_billing("Delivery Note", "dn_detail", "amount")` stops over-billing against a Delivery Note. ## How COGS is booked once 1. **The `update_stock` flag.** `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.make_item_gl_entries` (`gl_composer.py`, around line 374) adds the stock and COGS entries only if `cint(doc.update_stock) and erpnext.is_perpetual_inventory_enabled(company)`. It does this through `super(SalesInvoice, doc).get_gl_entries()`, which is `StockController`'s method. Likewise, `SalesInvoice.on_submit` calls `update_stock_ledger()` only when `update_stock == 1`. - An invoice made from a Delivery Note has `update_stock = 0`. It posts only income, receivable and tax entries, with no SLE and no COGS. 2. **`SalesInvoice.validate_delivery_note`.** If `update_stock` is on and any row has `delivery_note` set, it throws "Stock cannot be updated against the following Delivery Notes". An invoice therefore cannot move stock again for goods that are already delivered. - The other direction is covered by `Sales Invoice` → `mapper.make_delivery_note`. It only maps rows with `qty − delivered_qty > 0`, excludes rows that have `dn_detail`, and uses `delivered_qty`. 3. **Reversing SDBNB.** If the Delivery Note debited SDBNB, the invoice must still end up with COGS. - `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries` returns early if `update_stock` is set or perpetual inventory is off. Otherwise it handles each row that has `delivery_note` or `dn_detail`. - `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item` finds the Delivery Note's SLE (matched by `voucher_no`, `voucher_detail_no` and `item_code`). It takes the valuation rate from that SLE (`stock_value_difference / actual_qty`) and multiplies it by the invoiced `stock_qty`. - `_is_sdbnb_reversal` is true only when the Delivery Note's expense account has account type "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" and differs from the item's expense account. - `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries` then credits the Delivery Note's SDBNB account and debits the item's COGS account for that amount. - No new SLE is created. The invoice only reclassifies the value the Delivery Note already took out of stock. - Partial billing works because the amount uses the invoiced `stock_qty`, so the reclassified value matches the quantity billed. 4. **Status tracking.** `billed_amt`, `per_billed`, `delivered_qty` and `per_delivered` are recalculated from submitted documents on both submit and cancel. This keeps the mapped quantities consistent, so the same quantity is not delivered or billed twice. 5. **Returns and cancellation.** - `DeliveryNote.on_cancel` reverses its SLE and GL entries (`make_gl_entries_on_cancel`). - `SalesInvoice.make_gl_entries` calls `make_reverse_gl_entries` on cancel when `update_stock` is on and perpetual inventory is enabled. - Returns go through the same flags. If the company has no SDBNB account or has it switched off, the Delivery Note books Stock In Hand against COGS directly. The invoice then adds nothing for stock, because `update_stock` is 0 and no SDBNB reversal applies. COGS is booked once either way.

final answer
The flow runs in three stages. Order creation sets up the raw material (RM) requirement. A Stock Entry then sends the RM to the supplier. The Subcontracting Receipt consumes it and rolls the cost into the finished good. I traced the code but ran none of it. ## 1. Subcontracting Order (SCO) creation and RM requirement **File:** `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py` - **`SubcontractingOrder`** extends `SubcontractingController`. - `validate()` calls `validate_purchase_order_for_subcontracting`, `validate_service_items`, `validate_supplied_items` and `set_missing_values`. - `populate_items_table` builds the FG items from the PO service items. It takes the BOM from "Subcontracting BOM" or the item's default BOM. - `on_submit` calls `update_status`, `update_subcontracted_quantity_in_po` and `reserve_raw_materials`. The last of these makes Stock Reservation Entries through `StockReservation`. - `update_subcontracted_quantity_in_po` writes `subcontracted_qty` back to the Purchase Order Item. - **`SubcontractingController.validate`** in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py`: - It calls `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received`, which fills `supplied_items` (Subcontracting Order Supplied Item rows). - That in turn uses `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items`, `_get_materials_from_bom` and `__set_supplied_or_received_items`. - Quantities come from the BOM, or from the materials already transferred, depending on Buying Settings `backflush_raw_materials_of_subcontract_based_on`. - **Cost estimate on the SCO:** `calculate_supplied_items_qty_and_amount` sets `rm_cost_per_qty` from the BOM. `calculate_service_costs` and `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` then set `rate = rm + service + additional`. - **Status:** `update_status` moves through Open, Partial Material Transferred, Material Transferred, Partially Received and Completed. It uses `supplied_qty` against `required_qty` and `per_received`. The module-level `set_subcontracting_order_status` wraps it. ## 2. Transferring RM to the supplier warehouse - **`make_rm_stock_entry`** (whitelisted, `subcontracting_controller.py`) maps the SCO or PO to a Stock Entry with purpose "Send to Subcontractor". - Each row gets `s_warehouse` from the row's warehouse or `reserve_warehouse`, and `t_warehouse` from `supplier_warehouse`. - It sets `sco_rm_detail` (or `po_detail`) and `subcontracted_item`, and respects `over_transfer_allowance`. - `get_items_from_subcontract_order` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py` is a related helper. It calls `make_rm_stock_entry`. - **Stock Entry** (`erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/stock_entry.py`) uses `subcontract_data` to pick the right fields. The purpose class is **`SendToSubcontractorStockEntry`** in `services/subcontracting.py`. - `validate_subcontract_order` checks transfers against the order. It uses `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom` for BOM backflush and `validate_subcontracting_order_for_transfer` for transferred-material backflush. - Over-transfer is blocked using `get_total_supplied_qty`, `get_total_returned_qty` and the over-transfer allowance. `get_order_rm_detail` links the row to the SCO supplied item. - On submit or cancel, `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items` runs. It calls `get_supplied_items` to total `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty` and `total_supplied_qty` per `sco_rm_detail`, then writes them to the Subcontracting Order Supplied Item rows. It also calls `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting`, which updates the Bin. - `Stock Entry.update_subcontracting_order_status` calls `set_subcontracting_order_status`. It also reserves stock through `validate_closed_subcontracting_order` and the reservation code. - **Returns:** `make_return_stock_entry_for_subcontract` and `get_materials_from_supplier` (whitelisted) create a return Material Transfer. It uses `add_items_in_ste` and `get_available_materials`. ## 3. Receiving FG: Subcontracting Receipt (SCR) and cost roll-up **File:** `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`, class **`SubcontractingReceipt(SubcontractingController)`** - **Creating the SCR:** `make_subcontracting_receipt` calls `get_mapped_subcontracting_receipt` in the SCO file. The latter maps SCO to SCR, applies the BOM process-loss percentage and links `subcontracting_order_item`. - **Finding what is at the supplier** (`SubcontractingController`, `subcontracting_controller.py`): - `get_available_materials` builds the map of RM sitting at the supplier. - `__get_transferred_items` reads Stock Entries of purpose "Send to Subcontractor" and return Material Transfers. - `__update_consumed_materials` subtracts quantities already consumed by earlier SCRs. - **Choosing consumption:** `__set_supplied_or_received_items` and `__add_supplied_or_received_item` build the SCR `supplied_items` (Subcontracting Receipt Supplied Item) with `consumed_qty`. - Batch and serial numbers are handled by `set_batch_for_supplied_items` and `__set_serial_and_batch_bundle`. - `set_available_qty_for_consumption` in the SCR shows what is still available. - **Rates:** `set_valuation_rate_for_rm` calls `get_incoming_rate` against `supplier_warehouse`. `update_rate_for_supplied_items` and `set_rate_for_supplied_items` do the same per row. - **Validation:** the SCR's `validate` calls `reset_supplied_items`, `set_missing_values`, `get_current_stock`, `set_supplied_items_expense_account`, `set_supplied_items_cost_center` and `set_supplied_items_inventory_dimensions`. `validate_bom_required_qty` (called on submit) checks consumption against the BOM and raises `BOMQuantityError`. - **Cost roll-up:** - `calculate_additional_costs` (controller) distributes landed or additional costs by Amount or Qty into `additional_cost_per_qty`. - `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` (SCR) computes each supplied item's `amount = consumed_qty * rate`. - It sums these into `rm_supp_cost` and `rm_cost_per_qty` per FG row. - Secondary or scrap items give `secondary_items_cost_per_qty`. These come from `get_secondary_items`, `validate_secondary_items` and `remove_secondary_items`. - Then `item.rate = rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty + landed-cost-voucher cost per qty`. - The BOM's `cost_allocation_per` is applied to the amount. - Totals go into `total_qty` and `total`. `get_supplied_items_cost` returns the supplied-item cost for one FG row. - **On submit:** - `update_status_updater_args` and `update_prevdoc_status` update `received_qty` and `per_received` on the SCO and PO. - `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` calls `__update_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`. This writes `consumed_qty` back to the SCO supplied items, capped at `supplied_qty`. - `set_subcontracting_order_status` refreshes the SCO status. - `update_stock_ledger` first calls `update_ordered_and_reserved_qty`, which updates the SCO's ordered and reserved Bin quantities. It then builds the entries below, and `make_sl_entries` posts them: - The FG item gets an incoming SLE at the rolled-up `rate`, with `recalculate_rate`. - Rejected quantity goes to the rejected warehouse. - `make_sl_entries_for_supplier_warehouse` posts negative SLEs for the RM `consumed_qty` out of the supplier warehouse. - `make_gl_entries` uses `get_gl_entries`. `update_job_card`, `auto_create_purchase_receipt` and `update_status` follow. - On cancel, `on_cancel` reverses these. - **Related:** `SubcontractingController.update_stock_ledger` in `subcontracting_controller.py` and `Bin.update_reserved_qty_for_sub_contracting` handle the Bin side. ## Related doctypes - Subcontracting Order Item - Subcontracting Order Service Item - Subcontracting Order Supplied Item - Subcontracting Receipt Item - Subcontracting Receipt Supplied Item - Stock Entry Detail (`sco_rm_detail`, `subcontracted_item`)

final answer
The flow has three stages: transfer to the supplier, tracking on the Subcontracting Order, and receipt with cost roll-up. I read the receipt costing, the controller's stock-ledger code and the order-update hooks. I didn't open the rest, so I list the other names from search results without having read their bodies. ## 1. Subcontracting Order (SCO) defines what must be sent - **File:** `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py` - **Class:** `SubcontractingOrder(SubcontractingController)` - The finished-good items table is built by `populate_items_table`. - The required raw materials (the `supplied_items` table) come from the BOM or the transferred items. This is done in `SubcontractingController.validate`, `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received`, `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items` and `_get_materials_from_bom`, all in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py`. - `calculate_service_costs` and `calculate_supplied_items_qty_and_amount` total the service and raw-material amounts. - `validate_supplied_items` and `validate_service_items` check the order. Raw materials can be stock-reserved with `reserve_raw_materials` and `cancel_stock_reservation_entries`. - `update_ordered_qty_for_subcontracting` and `update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` update the Bin. `update_status` and the module functions `update_subcontracting_order_status` and `set_subcontracting_order_status` maintain the order status. ## 2. Sending raw materials to the supplier (Stock Entry, purpose "Send to Subcontractor") - **Creating the entry:** `make_rm_stock_entry` in `subcontracting_controller.py` maps the SCO's `supplied_items` (or a Purchase Order's) into a Stock Entry. - Its `post_process` sets `purpose = "Send to Subcontractor"`, links `subcontracting_order` and calls `set_stock_entry_type`. - It applies `Buying Settings.over_transfer_allowance`. - It links each row back through `sco_rm_detail` (or `po_detail`), using `add_items_in_ste` and `get_item_details`. - **Stock Entry service:** `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py`. - `validate_subcontract_order` calls `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom` and `validate_subcontracting_order_for_transfer`. - `get_order_rm_detail`, `_link_rm_detail_if_missing` and `get_total_supplied_qty` (with `_get_supplied_qty_order_filter`) find the matching order row and the quantity already supplied. - **Hook from the transfer entry:** `MaterialTransferStockEntry.on_submit` and `on_cancel` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py` call `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items`. That delegates to `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry.update_subcontract_order_supplied_items`. - **Tracking on the SCO:** `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items` does three things. - `_get_order_supplied_items` and `_get_supplied_items_details` (via module-level `get_supplied_items`) read the order rows and the submitted transfers. - `_update_supplied_items_in_order` writes `supplied_qty` onto the SCO's supplied-items rows. - `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` calls `get_bin` and `Bin.update_reserved_qty_for_sub_contracting` (`erpnext/stock/doctype/bin/bin.py`). - **Stock movement:** the entry moves stock from the company warehouse into the order's supplier warehouse. The warehouse's stock value stays on the company's books. - **Return and remaining quantities:** `make_return_stock_entry_for_subcontract`, `get_materials_from_supplier` and `get_pending_subcontracted_quantity` handle returns and pending quantities. The Script Report `buying/report/subcontracted_raw_materials_to_be_transferred` lists what is still to be sent. ## 3. Subcontracting Receipt (SCR): finished goods in, raw materials consumed - **Creating the receipt:** `make_subcontracting_receipt` and `get_mapped_subcontracting_receipt` in `subcontracting_order.py` map SCO items to an SCR. `update_item` sets `subcontracting_order_item`, the rates and the BOM. - **Document:** `SubcontractingReceipt(SubcontractingController)` in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`. Its child tables are `SubcontractingReceiptItem` (`items`), `SubcontractingReceiptSuppliedItem` (`supplied_items`) and `additional_costs` (Landed Cost Taxes and Charges). - **Updating the SCO:** `status_updater` in `__init__` updates `received_qty` and `per_received` on the Subcontracting Order Item and Subcontracting Order. `update_status_updater_args`, `update_status` and `validate_closed_subcontracting_order` support this. - **Building the consumed raw materials** (called from `before_validate`, `validate` and `set_missing_values`): - `reset_raw_materials`, `reset_supplied_items` and `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` rebuild the consumed rows. - Backflush is by BOM or by the quantities transferred. This is handled by `__get_transferred_items`, `__get_received_items`, `__get_consumed_items`, `get_available_materials` and `__add_supplied_or_received_item`. The setting is read by `__get_backflush_based_on`. - Batch and serial handling uses `set_batch_for_supplied_items` and `__set_serial_and_batch_bundle`. - `set_available_qty_for_consumption`, `get_current_stock`, `validate_bom_required_qty` and `get_rm_wise_consumed_qty` check availability and BOM quantity. `BOMQuantityError` is raised when the BOM quantity check fails. - **Raw-material rates:** `set_valuation_rate_for_rm`, `update_rate_for_supplied_items` and `set_rate_for_supplied_items` fill `rate` on the supplied items. `__set_rate_for_serial_and_batch_bundle` does the same for serial and batch bundles. - **Accounts and dimensions:** `set_supplied_items_expense_account`, `set_supplied_items_cost_center`, `set_supplied_items_inventory_dimensions`, `set_service_expense_account` and `set_expense_account_for_subcontracted_items`. - **Secondary items:** `get_secondary_items`, `validate_secondary_items`, `remove_secondary_items`, process loss and rejected quantities (`validate_accepted_warehouse`, `validate_rejected_warehouse`). ## 4. Cost roll-up The roll-up happens in `SubcontractingReceipt.calculate_items_qty_and_amount`, which `set_missing_values` calls. 1. Each supplied item gets `amount = consumed_qty * rate`. The amounts are summed per FG row through `reference_name` (`rm_cost_map`). `SubcontractingController.get_supplied_items_cost` does the same sum for a single row. 2. For each FG row, `rm_supp_cost` is set from that sum and `rm_cost_per_qty = rm_supp_cost / (received_qty or qty)`. 3. Secondary items (and legacy scrap) are summed into `secondary_items_cost_per_qty`. The code never subtracts this from the rate, so it is only recorded. 4. `rate = rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty + landed-cost-voucher cost per qty`. 5. If the row has a BOM, `amount` is also scaled by the BOM's `cost_allocation_per`. The method then sets `total_qty` and `total`. The service cost and additional cost per qty come from `SubcontractingController.calculate_additional_costs`, which distributes `additional_costs` by Qty or Amount (`distribute_additional_costs_based_on`). The SCO's `calculate_service_costs` supplies the service rate. ## 5. Stock ledger and GL on submit - **Submit and cancel:** `SubcontractingReceipt.on_submit` and `on_cancel` call `update_stock_ledger`, `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`, `update_ordered_and_reserved_qty`, `set_subcontracting_order_status` and `update_status`. They also call `update_job_card` when a job card is linked. - **Stock ledger:** `SubcontractingController.update_stock_ledger` writes the SLEs. - Finished goods are received into `item.warehouse` with `incoming_rate = item.rate` and `recalculate_rate = 1`. - Rejected quantity goes to `rejected_warehouse`. - `make_sl_entries_for_supplier_warehouse` issues each consumed raw material out of the supplier warehouse (`actual_qty = -consumed_qty`, `dependant_sle_voucher_detail_no = reference_name`). - **Back to the order:** `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` writes `consumed_qty` back onto the SCO's supplied-items rows through `__update_consumed_materials` and `__update_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`. It caps each row at `supplied_qty`. - **GL entries:** `SubcontractingReceipt.get_gl_entries` builds the entries with `SubcontractingReceiptGLComposer` (`subcontracting_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`: `compose` and `_make_item_gl_entries`). - It debits the FG warehouse's inventory account by the SLE `stock_value_difference`. - It credits the item's `expense_account` (the stock-received-but-not-billed side) by `stock_value_difference - service_cost`, where `service_cost = service_cost_per_qty * qty`. - It credits the `service_expense_account` (or the expense account) for the service cost. - The supplied-item amounts are grouped by `reference_name` for the raw-material entries. - **Reposting:** a later valuation change updates the supplied-item rates, so a repost recalculates the cost. `test_supplied_items_cost_after_reposting` in `test_subcontracting_receipt.py` covers this.

final answer
Raw materials move from your warehouse to the supplier's warehouse on a Stock Entry, get recorded on the Subcontracting Order, and are consumed and costed when the Subcontracting Receipt is submitted. I traced this from the code. I did not run it or read the full bodies of every function. **1. Subcontracting Order (the tracking document)** - Class `SubcontractingOrder` is in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py`. It extends `SubcontractingController` in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py`. - The controller's `validate`, `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items` and `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` build the `supplied_items` child table. That table is the doctype `Subcontracting Order Supplied Item`. - The raw material rows come from `_get_materials_from_bom`, using the BOM or the exploded BOM. `__add_supplied_or_received_item` and `set_rate_for_supplied_items` fill in each row and set its rate. - Each row records `required_qty`, `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty`, `total_supplied_qty`, `consumed_qty` and `reserve_warehouse`. - `SubcontractingOrder.reserve_raw_materials` creates Stock Reservation Entries when the order has `reserve_stock` set. - `SubcontractingOrder.update_status` sets the status from what has moved: - Open - Partial Material Transferred - Material Transferred - Partially Received - Completed - It also calls `update_requested_qty`, `update_ordered_qty_for_subcontracting` and `update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting`. **2. Transferring raw materials to the supplier** - `make_rm_stock_entry(subcontract_order, rm_items, order_doctype, target_doc)` is in `subcontracting_controller.py`. It is whitelisted and called from the order's "Material to Supplier" button. - It builds a Stock Entry with purpose "Send to Subcontractor" using `get_mapped_doc`. Its `post_process` step sets `subcontracting_order`, calls `set_stock_entry_type`, and fills the item rows. - Each item row has `s_warehouse` set to the row's `warehouse` or `reserve_warehouse`, and `t_warehouse` set to the order's `supplier_warehouse`. - Each row also carries `sco_rm_detail` (`po_detail` for a Purchase Order) and `subcontracted_item`. - The quantity is `required_qty` minus `total_supplied_qty`. `Buying Settings.over_transfer_allowance` limits over-transfer. - The helper `get_item_details` supplies item names and descriptions. - `Stock Entry` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/stock_entry.py`. For this purpose it uses the handler class `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py`. - `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry.validate` runs these checks: - `validate_subcontract_order` - `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom` - `_validate_transfer_qty` - `validate_subcontracting_order_for_transfer` - `get_total_supplied_qty` - `get_total_returned_qty` - `get_order_rm_detail` - On submit and cancel, `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items` runs. - It calls `get_supplied_items`, which sums the Stock Entry Detail `transfer_qty` per order row. - It writes `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty` and `total_supplied_qty` back to the order's supplied items. This is how the order tracks what has been sent. - It then calls `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting`, which updates the Bin's reserved quantity for subcontracting. - `StockEntry.on_submit` also calls `reserve_stock_for_subcontracting`, `update_wo_reservation_for_subcontracting` and `update_subcontracting_order_status`. The last one calls `set_subcontracting_order_status` on the order. **3. Returning materials** - `make_return_stock_entry_for_subcontract` and `add_items_in_ste` in the controller build a "Subcontracting Return" Stock Entry that sends materials back. - The order's "Return of components" action uses `get_materials_from_supplier`. - `get_items_from_subcontract_order` in the Stock Entry service file pulls the order's items into a Stock Entry. **4. Subcontracting Receipt** - `make_subcontracting_receipt` and `get_mapped_subcontracting_receipt` in `subcontracting_order.py` map the order into a receipt. They set `qty` to the order qty minus `received_qty`, and apply the BOM's `process_loss_percentage`. - `SubcontractingReceipt` is in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`. It also extends `SubcontractingController`. - `before_validate` runs these steps: - `validate_items_qty` - `set_items_bom` - `set_items_cost_center` - `set_service_expense_account` - `set_expense_account_for_subcontracted_items` - `validate` calls `reset_supplied_items`, then `set_missing_values`, then `get_current_stock`, which reads the Bin quantity at the supplier warehouse. It finishes with the `set_supplied_items_*` helpers for expense account, cost center and inventory dimensions. - `set_missing_values` runs `set_available_qty_for_consumption` and `calculate_items_qty_and_amount`. - The controller's `get_available_materials` and `__get_transferred_items` read the Stock Entries sent to the supplier. `__update_consumed_materials` and `__get_consumed_items` read what earlier receipts already used. - Consumption is set by `set_batch_for_supplied_items`, `__set_consumed_qty` and `__get_qty_based_on_material_transfer`. The backflush mode comes from `__get_backflush_based_on`: BOM, or Material Transferred for Subcontract. **5. Cost roll-up** - `SubcontractingReceipt.calculate_items_qty_and_amount` does the roll-up: - For each supplied item, `amount = consumed_qty * rate`. - These amounts are summed per finished-good row into `rm_supp_cost`, and `rm_cost_per_qty = rm_supp_cost / received_qty`. - Secondary or scrap items give `secondary_items_cost_per_qty`. - Each finished-good row's `rate` is `rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty + landed-cost-voucher cost per qty`. - `amount` is `received_qty * rate * BOM cost_allocation_per / 100`. - `set_valuation_rate_for_rm`, `update_rate_for_supplied_items` and `set_rate_for_supplied_items` set the rate of each raw material row. `get_supplied_items_cost` returns the raw material cost for one finished-good row. - `calculate_additional_costs` spreads the receipt's additional costs by amount or by quantity into `additional_cost_per_qty`. **6. Submit: stock, reconciliation and GL** - `SubcontractingReceipt.on_submit` runs these steps in order: 1. `validate_closed_subcontracting_order` 2. `validate_bom_required_qty` 3. `update_status_updater_args` 4. `update_prevdoc_status` 5. `set_subcontracting_order_status(update_bin=False)` 6. `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` 7. `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields` for both the `items` and `supplied_items` tables 8. `update_stock_reservation_entries` 9. `update_stock_ledger` 10. `make_gl_entries` 11. `repost_future_sle_and_gle` 12. `update_status` 13. `auto_create_purchase_receipt` 14. `update_job_card` - `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` is the reconciliation step. It calls `__update_consumed_materials` and `__update_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`, which write `consumed_qty` back to the order's supplied items. Each row is capped at its `supplied_qty`. - `update_stock_ledger` in the controller does the stock postings: - `update_ordered_and_reserved_qty` calls the order's `update_ordered_qty_for_subcontracting` and `update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting`. - Stock ledger entries add the finished goods at `incoming_rate = item.rate` into the accepted warehouse, and the rejected quantity into the rejected warehouse. - `make_sl_entries_for_supplier_warehouse` removes `consumed_qty` of each raw material from the supplier warehouse. - `get_gl_entries` builds the accounting entries, and `make_gl_entries` posts them. - `on_cancel` reverses these steps. - `set_subcontracting_order_status` then reloads each linked order and calls its `update_status`. This moves the order to Partially Received or Completed from `per_received`. **Other pieces in the flow** - Child doctypes: - `Subcontracting Receipt Supplied Item`, in `subcontracting_receipt_supplied_item/`. - `Purchase Receipt Item Supplied`, used when the receipt is made against a Purchase Order. - Reports: - `subcontracted_raw_materials_to_be_transferred` and `subcontract_order_summary`, which has its own `get_supplied_items`. - Tests: `erpnext/controllers/tests/test_subcontracting_controller.py`.

final answer
I traced this from two codegraph queries. Some functions I name below, in the "Not read" section at the end, I did not read in full. I took them from codegraph's symbol listings or from general knowledge of ERPNext. ## 1. Sending raw materials to the supplier **Creating the transfer.** `make_rm_stock_entry()` in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py` (line ~1344, whitelisted) builds the Stock Entry. - It takes the Subcontracting Order, or a Purchase Order via the `order_doctype` argument. It uses the order's `supplied_items`, or a list of items the user selects. - It maps the order to a Stock Entry with `get_mapped_doc` and sets `purpose = "Send to Subcontractor"`. Its `post_process` callback sets the `subcontracting_order` or `purchase_order` link and calls `set_stock_entry_type()`. - For each raw material it computes the quantity still to send: `required_qty - total_supplied_qty`. It respects `Buying Settings.over_transfer_allowance`. - Each row goes from `s_warehouse` (the row's `warehouse` or `reserve_warehouse`) to `t_warehouse` (the order's `supplier_warehouse`). - Each row also carries `sco_rm_detail` (or `po_detail` for a Purchase Order), `main_item_code`, `subcontracted_item` and any serial/batch fields. - `add_items_in_ste` (just after it) is a related helper. **Validating the transfer.** `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry(BaseStockEntry)` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py`. `stock_entry.py::_configure_purpose_class` chooses it for this purpose. - `validate()` calls `validate_subcontract_order()`. What it checks depends on `Buying Settings.backflush_raw_materials_of_subcontract_based_on`: - **BOM:** `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom` uses `_get_required_qty_for_bom` and `_validate_transfer_qty`. It blocks transfers above required quantity plus allowance, net of returns from `get_total_returned_qty`. It handles alternative items. `_link_rm_detail_if_missing` fills `sco_rm_detail` through `get_order_rm_detail`. - **Material Transferred for Subcontract:** `validate_subcontracting_order_for_transfer` requires `subcontracted_item` and links the order row. - `get_total_supplied_qty` sums the `transfer_qty` of submitted "Send to Subcontractor" entries. ## 2. Tracking against the Subcontracting Order - `on_submit` and `on_cancel` of the Stock Entry call `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items()`. - `_get_order_supplied_items` and `_get_supplied_items_details` (which calls `get_supplied_items()`) gather the quantities. - `_update_supplied_items_in_order` writes `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty` and `total_supplied_qty` onto each Subcontracting Order Supplied Item row (`SubcontractingOrderSuppliedItem`). - `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` calls `Bin.update_reserved_qty_for_sub_contracting()` on the reserve warehouse. - The Subcontracting Order itself is `SubcontractingOrder(SubcontractingController)` in `subcontracting_order.py`. It reuses `SubcontractingController.validate`, which calls `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received("supplied_items")`. - That calls `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items`, which runs the private steps `__identify_change_in_item_table`, `__prepare_supplied_or_received_items` and `__validate_supplied_or_received_items`. These build the required-materials table from the BOM or from the transferred stock. - `SubcontractingOrder.calculate_items_qty_and_amount` is in the same file, at line 206. - `SubcontractingService.update_subcontracting_order_status` is called from the Purchase Order and Sales Order status services. `SubcontractingController.set_subcontracting_order_status` calls `SubcontractingOrder.update_status`. This keeps the order's status and received percentage in sync. ## 3. Receiving finished goods through the Subcontracting Receipt `SubcontractingReceipt(SubcontractingController)` is in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`. **`validate()` (line 138):** - It calls `reset_supplied_items()`, then `super().validate()`. That runs the raw-material consumption setup and `set_valuation_rate_for_rm()`. - `set_valuation_rate_for_rm()` re-reads each consumed row's rate with `get_incoming_rate` from the supplier warehouse. If a rate changed, it calls `calculate_items_qty_and_amount()` again. - It then calls `set_missing_values()`, which runs `calculate_additional_costs()`. It also calls `get_secondary_items` (secondary and scrap items), the warehouse validations, `get_current_stock`, and the supplied-item expense account, cost centre and dimension setters. **Cost roll-up:** - `get_supplied_items_cost(item_row_id)` sums `consumed_qty * rate` of the supplied rows that reference a finished-goods row. The rate is the valuation rate of the material in the supplier warehouse. - `calculate_items_qty_and_amount()` (line 496) computes each finished item's amount and rate. Its body is not shown above. I take it to add raw-material cost, service cost and additional cost per unit. - `calculate_additional_costs()` (controller, line 1243) sums the `additional_costs` table. It distributes that total into `item.additional_cost_per_qty`, by Amount or by Qty depending on `distribute_additional_costs_based_on`. Secondary and scrap items are excluded. **`on_submit()` (line 168), in order:** 1. `validate_closed_subcontracting_order` 2. `validate_bom_required_qty` 3. `update_status_updater_args` and `update_prevdoc_status`, which update the received quantity on the order 4. `set_subcontracting_order_status(update_bin=False)` 5. `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`, which records consumed raw materials on the order 6. `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields` 7. `update_stock_reservation_entries` 8. `update_stock_ledger`, which creates the stock ledger entries. These receive the finished goods and consume the raw materials from the supplier warehouse. They go through `StockController.make_sl_entries`, then `StockLedgerService.make_sl_entries` and `stock_ledger.make_sl_entries`, which also updates batch quantities. 9. `make_gl_entries`, via `get_gl_entries`, which delegates to `SubcontractingReceiptGLComposer(self).compose()` in `subcontracting_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. This books finished-goods stock against the service and supplied-item expense accounts. 10. `repost_future_sle_and_gle` 11. `update_status`, which sets Completed, Return, Return Issued or Cancelled 12. `auto_create_purchase_receipt`, controlled by `Buying Settings.auto_create_purchase_receipt` 13. `update_job_card` ## Not read I did not read these in full. The names come from codegraph's symbol lists: - `SubcontractingReceipt.calculate_items_qty_and_amount` - `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` - `SubcontractingReceiptGLComposer` - the `landed_cost` and other methods in `SubcontractingOrder` I also did not open the supplied-item child doctypes `Subcontracting Receipt Supplied Item` and `Subcontracting Order Supplied Item`. They hold `required_qty`, `supplied_qty`, `consumed_qty`, `rate` and `reference_name`.

final answer
A Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) spreads its charges over the receipt items in `set_applicable_charges_on_item`. On submit, `update_landed_cost` then rewrites each receipt's valuation rate, stock ledger and GL entries. I read the code but did not run it. ## 1. Distributing the charges across items The code is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`, on the `LandedCostVoucher` class. - **Total to distribute:** `set_total_taxes_and_charges` sums `base_amount` over the `taxes` table into `total_taxes_and_charges`. - **Proportional split:** `set_applicable_charges_on_item` runs unless "Distribute Charges Based On" is "Distribute Manually". - The basis field is `frappe.scrub(distribute_charges_based_on)`, which is Qty, Amount, or a similar per-item column. - Each `Landed Cost Item` row gets `applicable_charges = item.<basis> × total_taxes_and_charges / Σ basis`. - Any rounding difference goes onto the last row. - It throws if the total basis is zero. - **Manual split:** `validate_applicable_charges_for_item` runs on submit. It checks that Σ `applicable_charges` equals `total_taxes_and_charges`. It absorbs a tiny rounding difference into the last row and throws on a larger mismatch. - **Row mapping:** `get_items_from_purchase_receipts` and `get_pr_items` load the receipt items into the voucher. Each row links back to the receipt line through `purchase_receipt_item` (or `stock_entry_item`). ## 2. Retroactively updating already-submitted receipts `on_submit` calls `validate_applicable_charges_for_item`, `update_landed_cost` and `update_claimed_landed_cost`. `on_cancel` runs the last two, which reverses the effect. `update_landed_cost` works in two passes over the `purchase_receipts` table. **Pass 1: update the rates** 1. It loads each receipt with `frappe.get_doc(receipt_document_type, receipt_document)`. 2. `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)` sums `applicable_charges` from the submitted `Landed Cost Item` rows for each receipt line. It matches on `purchase_receipt_item` (or `stock_entry_item` for a Stock Entry) and writes the sum to `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount`. 3. `update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py` recalculates each line's rate. The formula is `valuation_rate = (base_net_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount + amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice) / (qty × conversion_factor)`. - For a Subcontracting Receipt it calls `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` instead. 4. `item.db_update()` saves the new values on the already-submitted document. 5. `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items` sets `Serial No.purchase_rate` to the new valuation rate. Stock Entries and Subcontracting Receipts skip this step. **Pass 2: repost the stock ledger and GL** 1. It sets `docstatus = 2` and calls `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`, then `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`. This reverses the old stock ledger and GL entries. 2. It sets `docstatus = 1` and calls these in order: - `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` - `update_stock_ledger(...)` again, now at the new rate - `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` for a Purchase Receipt, or plain `make_gl_entries()` for other receipt types 3. `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` reposts later stock ledger and GL entries, so later issues pick up the new valuation. It is defined in `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py` and delegates to `StockLedgerService`. ## 3. How the GL entries book the charges - **Entry composer:** `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` delegates to `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer.compose` in `stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. - It includes `landed_cost_voucher_amount` in the stock-in-hand debit. - It calls the inner `make_landed_cost_gl_entries` to credit the expense accounts. - Fixed-asset lines with a landed cost amount get separate handling (`d.is_fixed_asset and d.landed_cost_voucher_amount`). - **Per-account amounts:** `StockController.get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries` in `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py` calls the module function of the same name in `landed_cost_voucher.py`. - That function finds the submitted vouchers through `Landed Cost Purchase Receipt` rows. - For each voucher it builds a map `{(item_code, receipt_item_name): {expense_account: {amount, base_amount}}}`. Each item's share of every charge account is prorated by the voucher's distribution basis. - **Helpers:** `has_landed_cost_amount` is a quick check that a receipt has any landed cost. `PurchaseReceipt.is_landed_cost_booked_for_any_item` does the same on the document.

final answer
The Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) spreads its charges across receipt lines, writes each line's share back onto the receipt, and then reposts the receipt's stock ledger and GL entries. I read the code but did not run any of it. All of the LCV code is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`, class `LandedCostVoucher`. ## 1. Distributing the charges across line items - **Collect the lines.** `get_items_from_purchase_receipts()` runs when the user clicks the button, or from `validate()` if the items table is empty. For every receipt in `purchase_receipts`, it appends a `Landed Cost Item` row per receipt line. Each row copies item_code, qty, rate, `base_amount`, cost center and `is_fixed_asset`. It also stores the receipt reference in `purchase_receipt_item`, or in `stock_entry_item` for a Stock Entry. - **Total the charges.** `validate()` calls `set_total_taxes_and_charges()`, which sums `base_amount` over the `taxes` table (freight, customs and so on) into `total_taxes_and_charges`. - **Split them.** `set_applicable_charges_on_item()` does the split, unless `distribute_charges_based_on` is "Distribute Manually". - The basis field is Qty or Amount. - Each line gets `applicable_charges = line_basis × total_taxes_and_charges / sum(basis)`, rounded to the field's precision. - Any rounding remainder is added to the last line. - It throws an error if the total basis is zero. - The JS handler `distribute_charges_based_on` → `set_applicable_charges_for_item` in `landed_cost_voucher.js` does the same on the client. - **Check on submit.** `on_submit()` first calls `validate_applicable_charges_for_item()`. That check makes sure the per-line charges add up to `total_taxes_and_charges`. A difference within about 2 units of the field's last decimal place is absorbed into the last row, and a larger one raises an error. This is the check that matters for manual distribution. ## 2. Updating receipts that were already submitted `on_submit()` and `on_cancel()` both call `update_landed_cost()`. It works in two passes over `purchase_receipts`. **Pass 1: recompute valuation on the receipt lines.** - `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)` is a module-level function in the same file. For each receipt line it sums `Landed Cost Item.applicable_charges` over all submitted LCVs (docstatus 1) that point at that line. It writes the sum to the line's `landed_cost_voucher_amount`. A cancelled LCV drops out of the sum, which is why the same method serves cancel. - `doc.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` is in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`. It recomputes each line's `valuation_rate` as `(base_net_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount + amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice) / (qty × conversion_factor)`. - For a Subcontracting Receipt, `calculate_items_qty_and_amount()` is called instead. - Stock Entries use their own `update_valuation_rate()` in `stock_entry.py`. - Each line is saved straight to the database with `item.db_update()`, so the submitted document is not resubmitted. - `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items()` sets `Serial No.purchase_rate` to the new valuation rate. **Pass 2: reverse and repost the stock ledger and GL.** For each receipt, the code reloads the document and: 1. Sets `docstatus = 2`. It then calls `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` and `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`. This reverses the old stock ledger and GL entries. 2. Sets `docstatus = 1`. It then calls `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` and `update_stock_ledger(...)` again, which rebuilds the stock ledger entries with the new valuation rate. 3. Calls `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` for a Purchase Receipt, or plain `make_gl_entries()` for other receipt types. 4. Calls `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. This reposts later stock ledger entries and GL entries so that downstream valuation, such as stock issued after the receipt, is corrected. ## 3. How the GL entries pick up the charges The Purchase Receipt GL composer is `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. - `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries(doc)` in the LCV file builds a map from (item, receipt line) to expense account and amount. It reads the submitted `Landed Cost Purchase Receipt` and `Landed Cost Taxes and Charges` rows. - `make_landed_cost_gl_entries(item)` in the composer then credits each LCV expense account for its share. The matching increase goes to the stock-in-hand account through the valuation amount, which now includes `landed_cost_voucher_amount`. - `make_divisional_loss_gl_entry` includes `landed_cost_voucher_amount` when it compares the document's valuation with the stock value difference. - Purchase Invoice and Subcontracting Receipt have their own `gl_composer.py` files that do the same.

final answer
A Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) first splits its charges across the receipt items. On submit or cancel, it then rewrites the valuation rate on each linked receipt and re-posts that receipt's stock ledger and GL entries. I read the LCV file (`landed_cost_voucher.py`) and grepped `buying_controller.py` for the landed-cost references. I did not read the body of `update_valuation_rate` or of `Purchase Receipt.make_gl_entries`. **1. Distributing charges across line items** - The code is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`, on the `LandedCostVoucher` class. - `get_items_from_purchase_receipts` and `get_pr_items` load the receipt lines into the LCV's `items` table, which holds `Landed Cost Item` rows. - `set_total_taxes_and_charges` sums `base_amount` across the `taxes` table (freight, customs and similar) into `total_taxes_and_charges`. - `set_applicable_charges_on_item` does the split, unless `distribute_charges_based_on` is "Distribute Manually". - The basis field is Qty, Amount or similar, looked up with `frappe.scrub`. - Each item gets `applicable_charges = basis_value × total_taxes_and_charges / total_basis`. - Any rounding difference goes onto the last item. - If the basis totals zero, it throws an error. - `validate_applicable_charges_for_item` runs on submit. It checks that the item charges add up to `total_taxes_and_charges`. A rounding difference below about 2 units of precision is absorbed into the last row. Anything larger throws an error. With manual distribution, the user's own figures are the proportions. **2. Retroactively updating already-submitted receipts** - `on_submit` and `on_cancel` both call `update_landed_cost`, followed by `update_claimed_landed_cost`. That second method sets `claimed_landed_cost_amount` on the vendor invoices (back to 0 on cancel). - `update_landed_cost` runs two passes over `purchase_receipts`. The receipt document can be a Purchase Receipt, Purchase Invoice, Subcontracting Receipt or Stock Entry. - **Pass 1, valuation:** - `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)` is a module-level function. For each receipt item it sums `applicable_charges` from all submitted (`docstatus` 1) `Landed Cost Item` rows that match the receipt and the item row. The match is on `purchase_receipt_item`, or `stock_entry_item` for a Stock Entry. The result goes into `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount`. Because it sums only submitted LCVs, a cancel automatically drops that LCV's share. - `doc.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` is defined in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py` (line 414). It adds `landed_cost_voucher_amount` into the item's valuation amount, near line 484. Subcontracting Receipts call `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` instead. - `item.db_update()` saves the new values directly to the database. - `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items` writes the new `valuation_rate` to `purchase_rate` on the serial numbers. Stock Entries and Subcontracting Receipts skip this step. - **Pass 2, ledgers:** each receipt is reloaded and its stock and GL postings are redone. - It sets `docstatus = 2` and calls `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`, then `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`. This reverses the old postings. - It sets `docstatus = 1` and calls `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`, then `update_stock_ledger(...)` again, which posts the stock ledger entries at the new rate. - For a Purchase Receipt it then calls `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. Other receipt types call `make_gl_entries()`. - Finally `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` re-values later stock transactions and their GL entries. - `update_stock_ledger` is defined at `buying_controller.py` line 754 and passes the `via_landed_cost_voucher` flag down to the stock ledger entry creation. **3. GL accounting for the charges** - `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries(doc)`, also in `landed_cost_voucher.py`, builds a map of `(item_code, row)` → expense account → amount and base amount. It reads the submitted LCVs linked to the receipt and splits each tax row across items in proportion to the same basis. This is what the receipt's GL composer uses to credit the LCV expense accounts and debit stock, so the repost carries the extra cost. - `has_landed_cost_amount` gates this map. It returns early when no item has a landed cost amount. Other pieces: - `LandedCostPurchaseReceipt` and `LandedCostTaxesandCharges` are the child doctypes. - `validate_receipt_documents` and `validate_asset_qty_and_status` run checks beforehand.

final answer
A Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) does two jobs. It first splits the charges across the voucher's item rows. When it is submitted, it then rewrites the already-submitted receipts' stock and GL postings. The code is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`, class `LandedCostVoucher`. I read the LCV file and grepped for the `via_landed_cost_voucher` flag. I did not open the downstream implementations, so those steps are described from their names and call sites. ## 1. Redistributing the charges across items - **`get_items_from_purchase_receipts`** fills the LCV `items` table (`Landed Cost Item`) from the linked receipts. It calls `get_pr_items` for each receipt. - **`set_total_taxes_and_charges`** sets `total_taxes_and_charges` to the sum of `base_amount` over the `taxes` rows (the freight, customs and similar charges). - **`set_applicable_charges_on_item`** does the split, unless `distribute_charges_based_on` is "Distribute Manually": - The basis field is the scrubbed setting, for example `qty`, `amount` or `weight`. The function sums that field over all items. - Each item's `applicable_charges` is its share of the basis multiplied by `total_taxes_and_charges / total_item_cost`, rounded to the field's precision. - Any rounding difference is added to the last item, so the rows sum exactly to the total. - If the basis total is zero, it throws an error. - **`validate_applicable_charges_for_item`** runs on submit. It checks that the item charges add up to `total_taxes_and_charges`. This also covers manual distribution. A small difference (under about 2 units of the last decimal place) is absorbed into the last row. A larger difference throws an error. ## 2. Retroactively updating the submitted receipts `on_submit` calls `validate_applicable_charges_for_item`, then `update_landed_cost`, then `update_claimed_landed_cost`. `on_cancel` calls `update_landed_cost` again. On cancel the voucher is no longer submitted, so its charges drop out of the sums below and the receipts return to their previous values. `update_landed_cost` works in two passes over the `purchase_receipts` table. **Pass 1: change the receipt items' valuation.** 1. It loads the original receipt with `frappe.get_doc(receipt_document_type, receipt_document)`. 2. On submit, `validate_asset_qty_and_status` checks any fixed-asset items. 3. `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)` is a module-level function. For each receipt item it sums `applicable_charges` over all submitted `Landed Cost Item` rows (`docstatus == 1`) linked to that item. The link is `purchase_receipt_item`, or `stock_entry_item` for a Stock Entry. The sum goes into `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount`, so several LCVs on one receipt accumulate. 4. `doc.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` recomputes the item's `valuation_rate` to include the landed cost. A Subcontracting Receipt calls `calculate_items_qty_and_amount()` instead. 5. `item.db_update()` writes the new amounts straight to the database. The submitted document is not re-saved. 6. For receipts other than Stock Entry and Subcontracting Receipt, `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items` refreshes the serial-number rates. **Pass 2: re-post the ledgers.** For each receipt it reloads the document and does the following: 1. It sets `docstatus = 2`, then calls `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` and `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`. This reverses the old Stock Ledger Entries and GL entries. `make_gl_entries_on_cancel` is in `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`. 2. It sets `docstatus = 1` and calls `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. It then calls `update_stock_ledger(...)` again, which writes new SLEs at the updated valuation rate. 3. It rebuilds the GL entries. For a Purchase Receipt this is `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. Other receipt types use plain `make_gl_entries()`. 4. `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` re-posts later transactions of the same items, so downstream stock values and COGS follow the new rate. ## 3. Where the flag is used - `StockController.make_gl_entries` (`erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`, line 178) passes the flag to `get_gl_entries`. - `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries` (`erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py`, line 478) passes it to `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer.compose`. - That composer is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. Its `_make_tax_gl_entries` takes the flag so it can handle the landed-cost amounts. This is the step that books the charges into the GL, with the expense accounts credited and the stock-in-hand account debited. - `StockController.make_sl_entries` and `repost_future_sle_and_gle` (which hands off to `StockLedgerService`) also take the flag. The repost record in `stock_controller.py` stores it as `via_landed_cost_voucher`. The Stock Ledger Entry and Serial and Batch Bundle code check it, for example around line 184 of `stock_ledger_entry.py` and line 948 of `serial_and_batch_bundle.py`. I did not read those checks, so I can't say exactly what they skip or allow.
