final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints with the `methods` argument of Frappe's whitelist decorator: `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])`. I found the decorator usage by searching the repo and did not read Frappe's source. The enforcement description below therefore comes from my knowledge of Frappe. **Mechanism** - `frappe.whitelist(allow_guest=False, xss_safe=False, methods=None)` registers a function as a remotely callable `/api/method/...` endpoint. - `methods` lists the allowed HTTP verbs. The default is all of them, including GET. - When a request is dispatched, Frappe's `frappe.handler` and `frappe.is_whitelisted` check the request method against that list. If the verb isn't allowed, Frappe raises `frappe.PermissionError` (HTTP 403 in current versions) before the function body runs. - With `methods=["POST"]`, a GET request is rejected. - This is a CSRF-style safeguard. It also stops the endpoint being triggered by a link, an image tag or a prefetch. POST requests also go through Frappe's CSRF-token check. - The same argument is used on instance methods of controller classes, such as `Issue`, `Customer` and `Workstation`. The restriction also covers whitelisted document methods called through `run_doc_method`. **Concrete endpoints with `methods=["POST"]`** - `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`: `update_company_master_and_address` - `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`: `make_quality_inspections` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/lead/lead.py`: `add_lead_to_prospect` - `erpnext/stock/doctype/material_request/material_request.py`: `raise_work_orders` - `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py`: `make_work_orders` - `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/work_order.py`: `set_work_order_ops` - `erpnext/projects/doctype/task/task.py`: `set_multiple_status`, `add_node` and `add_multiple_tasks` More endpoints carry the same decorator in these files: - `erpnext/crm/doctype/lead/mapper.py` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/opportunity/mapper.py` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/opportunity/opportunity.py` - `erpnext/setup/doctype/company/company.py` - `erpnext/setup/doctype/employee/employee.py` - `erpnext/stock/doctype/batch/batch.py` - `erpnext/stock/doctype/warehouse/warehouse.py` - `erpnext/projects/doctype/project/project.py` - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` - `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py` - `erpnext/www/banking.py`, which also uses `allow_guest=True` My search output was cut off at 40 results, so there are likely more such endpoints than I listed.

final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints with the `methods` argument of Frappe's whitelist decorator. I found this by searching the code. I didn't open Frappe's own source, so what happens when a GET hits a POST-only endpoint comes from my knowledge of Frappe, not from this repo. **Mechanism** State-changing endpoints are declared with `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])` instead of plain `@frappe.whitelist()`. The decorator records the allowed HTTP verbs on the function. When a request comes in through `/api/method/<dotted.path>` or `frappe.call`, Frappe's request handler checks the request's HTTP verb against that list. If the verb isn't allowed, Frappe raises a `PermissionError` (HTTP 403) before the function body runs. GET requests are the ones that need blocking, because they can be triggered cross-site through links or images. This restriction is also enforced at the server, so it doesn't depend on how the client behaves. Read-only endpoints are sometimes marked explicitly with `methods=["GET"]`. Examples are two functions in `bank_reconciliation_tool.py`, at lines 388 and 900. **Concrete endpoints with `methods=["POST"]`** - `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/work_order.py`: `set_work_order_ops(name)` loads a Work Order and calls `save()`. - `erpnext/projects/doctype/task/task.py`: `set_multiple_status(names, status)` saves each Task with the new status. The first search returned this function as a match for `methods=["POST"]`, so I believe it carries the decorator. The grep output I have (capped at 40 lines) doesn't show it, so I haven't confirmed that. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py`: `make_merge_log(invoices)` creates a POS Invoice Merge Log document. It has the same caveat as `set_multiple_status`. - `erpnext/accounts/bulk_payment.py`: line 12. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_reconciliation_tool/bank_reconciliation_tool.py`: many POST-only functions, at lines 119, 149, 308, 459, 503, 580, 673, 771, 867 and 1060. - `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`: line 1654. - `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`: line 658. - `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py`: line 843. - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py`: line 25. - `erpnext/setup/doctype/company/company.py`: lines 1012 and 1123. - `erpnext/setup/doctype/employee/employee.py`: line 435. - `erpnext/setup/doctype/department/department.py`: line 98. - `erpnext/stock/doctype/warehouse/warehouse.py`: line 194. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`: line 881. - `erpnext/support/doctype/issue/issue.py`: lines 120 (a method on the Issue doctype class) and 276. - `erpnext/support/doctype/service_level_agreement/service_level_agreement.py`: line 785. - `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py`: lines 53 and 82. - `erpnext/stock/report/stock_ledger_invariant_check/stock_ledger_invariant_check.py`: line 309. When a request reaches `/api/method/<dotted.path>`, Frappe's request handler checks the request verb against that list. - A GET request to a POST-only function is rejected with a `PermissionError` / 405 "Method Not Allowed". final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints with the `methods` argument of Frappe's whitelist decorator: `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])`. I found this by grepping the repo for the decorator and reading the function names beside it. I did not open Frappe's source, so the enforcement details below come from my knowledge of Frappe. **Mechanism** - A plain `@frappe.whitelist()` function can be called through `/api/method/<dotted.path>` with GET or POST. - When you pass `methods=[...]`, Frappe records the allowed HTTP verbs for that function. - When a request arrives, Frappe checks the request's verb against that list. If the verb isn't allowed, it raises `frappe.PermissionError`/`frappe.BadRequest`, and the endpoint answers with a 405 "Method Not Allowed" before the function body runs. - A GET request is therefore rejected for these endpoints, and the data can only change through a POST. - POST requests also go through Frappe's CSRF-token check. This is the security benefit: a GET can't be triggered by an `<img>` tag, a link, or a cross-site navigation. **Endpoints that carry `methods=["POST"]`** - `erpnext/buying/doctype/request_for_quotation/mapper.py`: `create_supplier_quotation`. The supplier portal's `rfq.js` calls it with `type: "POST"`. - `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py`: `make_work_orders`. - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py`: `make_purchase_receipt`. - `erpnext/crm/doctype/lead/mapper.py`: `make_lead_from_communication`. - `erpnext/crm/doctype/opportunity/mapper.py`: `make_opportunity_from_communication`. - `erpnext/accounts/bulk_payment.py`: `create_payment_entries`. - `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/work_order.py`: `set_work_order_ops`. - `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`: `make_quality_inspections`. - `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`: `update_company_master_and_address`. - `erpnext/projects/doctype/task/task.py`: `set_multiple_status`, `add_node` and `add_multiple_tasks`. - `erpnext/setup/doctype/company/company.py`: `add_node` and `create_transaction_deletion_request`. - Other files in the same grep results: `assets/doctype/location/location.py`, `manufacturing/doctype/workstation/workstation.py`, `support/doctype/issue/issue.py`, `accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`, `setup/doctype/employee/employee.py`, `projects/doctype/project/project.py`, `setup/doctype/department/department.py`, and `bisect_accounting_statements.py`. The grep output was cut off at 25 matches, so these examples are not a complete list. Some of the decorators sit on class methods, for example in `workstation.py`, `issue.py` and `bisect_accounting_statements.py`, so the restriction also applies to whitelisted document methods.

When raw materials for a Work Order are transferred in several partial batches (e.g. via pick lists that could only be partially fulfilled), how does ERPNext compute the work order's "material transferred for manufacturing" progress, and what prevents transferring more material than the work order requires? Name the relevant functions and files.

final answer
When raw materials reach a Work Order in several partial batches, ERPNext recomputes `material_transferred_for_manufacturing` from the submitted Stock Entries each time. It then limits further transfers by comparing each item's requirement with what has already been moved. I read these files directly but did not run any of this. **How the progress is computed** The code is in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/required_items.py`, in `RequiredItemsService`. `WorkOrder.refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` in `work_order.py` delegates to it. 1. `update_transferred_qty_for_required_items` first calls `_material_transfer_qty_by_item(is_return=0)`. That sums the transferred quantity per item over the submitted "Material Transfer for Manufacture" Stock Entries for this work order. 2. It writes each total into the matching required-items row's `transferred_qty` with `db_set`. 3. It then calls `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing(transferred_items)`. 4. `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` runs only the recompute step. `StatusService` in `services/status.py` calls it before deciding between Not Started and In Process. `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` has three cases: - **Job Card:** if the work order has operations and `transfer_material_against == "Job Card"`, it returns without changing anything. The Job Card code in `job_card.py` sets the value instead, using the minimum completed quantity across operations. - **Stock Entries with `fg_completed_qty`:** it takes `StatusService.get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty("Material Transfer for Manufacture", "material_transferred_for_manufacturing")` from `status.py`. That is `SUM(fg_completed_qty)` over submitted, non-additional-transfer entries. If the sum is non-zero, it is stored and the function stops. - **Pick-list transfers:** these leave `fg_completed_qty` at 0, so the function uses a minimum fraction. For each item it totals `required_qty` over rows that have `include_item_in_manufacturing` set and a positive quantity. It then takes the smallest `transferred / required` ratio across items, capped at 1.0. The result is `min_fraction * work_order.qty`, written with `db_set`. With the minimum-fraction rule, one item that is only partly fulfilled holds the progress at that fraction of the work order quantity. The work order is not marked fully transferred just because the other items were completely transferred. **What stops over-transfer** The checks are in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py`, in `MaterialTransferForManufactureStockEntry`. - **`_validate_no_excess_transfer`:** this runs from `validate_component_and_quantities` when the entry has a work order and no `fg_completed_qty`, which is the pick-list case. - For each item it computes pending as `required_qty - transferred_qty`, summed across the required-items rows. - It sums the quantity being moved out of a source warehouse for that item, mapping substitute items back through `original_item`. - If the transfer is larger than pending, it throws "Excess Material Transfer… Maximum transferable quantity is …". - It skips return entries. It also skips the check when Manufacturing Settings `backflush_raw_materials_based_on` is "Material Transferred for Manufacture". - **`get_pending_raw_materials`:** this runs when items are auto-added to a transfer entry. `_calculate_item_transfer_qty` limits each item to `required_qty - transferred_qty`, and `_resolve_transfer_qty` makes the final choice. The quantity is allowed to go higher only in two cases. One is when the backflush setting above applies. The other is when `_is_overproduction_allowed` says the overall quantity stays within `qty` plus the percentage from "Transfer Extra Materials Percentage" (or the overproduction percentage if that is unset) in Manufacturing Settings. If every item is already fully transferred, the user sees "All items have already been transferred for this Work Order." - **Additional transfers:** `work_order.py` around line 599 throws an error when `additional_transferred_qty` is greater than the allowed extra percentage.

final answer
**Computing the progress** The work is in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/required_items.py`, in `RequiredItemsService.recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing(transferred_items)`. Two other methods in the same class, `update_transferred_qty_for_required_items` and `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`, call it. I read only this function and `_validate_no_excess_transfer`, not their callers. The function works in three steps: 1. **Job Card transfers.** If the work order has operations and `transfer_material_against == "Job Card"`, it returns without changing anything. In that case the figure comes from the job cards instead, using the minimum completed quantity across operations. 2. **Stock Entries that set `fg_completed_qty`.** It calls `StatusService.get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty("Material Transfer for Manufacture", "material_transferred_for_manufacturing")`. That is in `work_order/services/status.py` and sums `fg_completed_qty` over the submitted transfer Stock Entries. If the sum is non-zero, the function stores it with `db_set` and stops. Direct entries and excess-transfer tracking use this path. 3. **Pick-list partial transfers.** The pick list flow sets `fg_completed_qty` to 0, so the sum from step 2 is 0 and the function falls through to this step. - It builds `required_by_item`, which adds up `required_qty` per item over the required-item rows that are included in manufacturing and have a positive quantity. - For each item it takes `transferred_items[item] / required_qty`, where `transferred_items` holds the actually transferred quantities per item. - It takes the smallest of these fractions and caps it at 1.0. - It sets `material_transferred_for_manufacturing = min_fraction * work_order.qty`. The limiting item sets the progress, so a partial transfer doesn't mark the work order as fully transferred. The per-item `transferred_qty` on each required item is refreshed by `update_transferred_qty_for_required_items` in the same class. **Preventing over-transfer** - **Stock Entry check.** `MaterialTransferForManufactureStockEntry._validate_no_excess_transfer` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py`. `validate_component_and_quantities` calls it. - For each item it computes the pending quantity as the sum of `required_qty - transferred_qty` over the work order's `required_items`. - It adds up the quantities of the stock entry rows that have a source warehouse. A row for a substitute item is counted against the original item through `original_item`. - If the total for an item exceeds the pending quantity, it throws "Row #: Cannot transfer X. Maximum transferable quantity is Y" with the title "Excess Material Transfer". - The check is skipped for returns. - It is also skipped when Manufacturing Settings has `backflush_raw_materials_based_on = "Material Transferred for Manufacture"`. - **Work order check.** `StatusService.update_work_order_qty` in `work_order/services/status.py` runs after submit and calls `WorkOrder.validate_additional_transferred_qty` when `additional_transferred_qty` is set. It handles transfers that go beyond the planned quantity. - **Submit and cancel.** `MaterialTransferForManufactureStockEntry.update_job_card_and_work_order` calls `_validate_work_order`, then `update_work_order_qty` and `update_status`.

final answer
**How the progress is computed** The code is in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/`. 1. `StatusService.update_work_order_qty` in `status.py` is the entry point. It runs when a Stock Entry is submitted or cancelled. For each purpose in `_QTY_PURPOSES`, `_update_qty_for_purpose` calls `get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty("Material Transfer for Manufacture", "material_transferred_for_manufacturing")`. - That function sums `fg_completed_qty` over submitted Stock Entries for the work order with that purpose. - The result is saved with `db_set`. - Job Card–based transfers are skipped here by `_skip_transfer_purpose`. They are handled in `job_card.py`, around line 1271, which uses the minimum completed qty across operations. 2. `RequiredItemsService.update_transferred_qty_for_required_items` in `required_items.py` handles the item-level side. - `_material_transfer_qty_by_item` sums `Stock Entry Detail.transfer_qty` per item. It uses submitted, non-return "Material Transfer for Manufacture" entries linked to the work order. - It groups by `(item_code, original_item)`, so substitute items are credited to the item they replace. - The totals are written to each `required_items` row's `transferred_qty`. - It then calls `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. 3. `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` is where partial pick-list batches are handled. - If the summed `fg_completed_qty` is non-zero, that sum is used. - Pick-list transfers set `fg_completed_qty = 0`, so they fall through to a "min-fraction" calculation. - For each required item it computes `transferred / required_qty`. - It takes the minimum of those fractions, capped at 1.0, and multiplies it by `work_order.qty`. - This means a work order is not counted as fully transferred while any one raw material is still short. - `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` runs the same recompute and is called from `StatusService`. The status decision (Not Started vs In Process) therefore uses fresh data. **What prevents over-transfer** - **Hard cap on the work order total.** `_update_qty_for_purpose` in `status.py` raises `StockOverProductionError` if the total exceeds `qty + allowance%`. The error reads "… cannot be greater than planned quantity …". - The allowance is `overproduction_percentage_for_work_order` from Manufacturing Settings. - For transfers it falls back to `transfer_extra_materials_percentage` (`_qty_allowance`). - `validate_additional_transferred_qty` in `work_order.py` is also called when there is `additional_transferred_qty`. - **Cap when the Stock Entry is built.** `get_pending_raw_materials` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py` limits what a new entry proposes. - `_calculate_item_transfer_qty` works out `pending_to_issue = required_qty - transferred_qty`. - It also works out `desire_to_transfer = fg_completed_qty * required_qty / wo.qty`. - `_resolve_transfer_qty` then picks the quantity. Transfers beyond the pending quantity are only allowed if `_is_overproduction_allowed` passes. That check allows up to `qty * (1 + pct/100)`, measured against `material_transferred_for_manufacturing + fg_completed_qty`. - If nothing is left, it shows "All items have already been transferred for this Work Order." - **Candidate filter.** The pick-list query in `pick_list.py`, around line 1350, only selects work orders where `qty > material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. I did not read `_resolve_transfer_qty` itself, so its exact capping behaviour is inferred from how it is called.

final answer
Still checking how the transferred quantity is summed and capped. I have the main pieces, so I'll finish with the answer. **How the progress is computed** The progress field is `Work Order.material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. It is set in two places. 1. **Sum of `fg_completed_qty`.** `StatusService.update_work_order_qty` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/status.py` loops over `_QTY_PURPOSES`. For "Material Transfer for Manufacture", `_update_qty_for_purpose` calls `get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty`. That runs an SQL `Sum` over submitted Stock Entries for the work order, and for transfers it sums `fg_completed_qty`. The result is saved with `db_set`. 2. **Partial pick-list transfers.** These entries have `fg_completed_qty = 0`, so the sum above is 0. `RequiredItemsService.recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/required_items.py` then takes over. It is called from `update_transferred_qty_for_required_items` and `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. - `_material_transfer_qty_by_item(is_return=0)` totals the actually transferred quantity per item from submitted Stock Entry details. This also feeds each required item row's `transferred_qty`. - It builds `required_by_item`, the required quantity per item. It skips rows where `include_item_in_manufacturing` is off or `required_qty` is 0. - It takes `min_fraction`, the smallest value of transferred ÷ required across all items, capped at 1.0. - It stores `min_fraction × work_order.qty` with `db_set`. Because it uses the minimum, a work order isn't shown as fully transferred while any one raw material is still short. **Edge cases and status** - If `transfer_material_against == "Job Card"`, the recompute returns early and Job Cards track the quantity instead. `_skip_transfer_purpose` skips the same case in the status service. - `StatusService` calls `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` before deciding the status. This stops a pick-list-only transfer from leaving the work order at "Not Started". **What prevents over-transfer** - **Work order level cap:** `_update_qty_for_purpose` throws `StockOverProductionError` when the transferred total exceeds `qty + allowance% × qty`. The message is "… cannot be greater than planned quantity …". - `_qty_allowance` reads `overproduction_percentage_for_work_order` from Manufacturing Settings. If that is 0, it falls back to `transfer_extra_materials_percentage` for transfers. - **Additional transfers:** `WorkOrder.validate_additional_transferred_qty` in `work_order.py` checks `material_transferred_for_manufacturing + additional_transferred_qty` against `qty + transfer_extra_materials_percentage%`. It throws if the total is higher. - **Stock Entry quantity proposal:** in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py`, `MaterialTransferForManufactureStockEntry.get_pending_raw_materials` limits each item's quantity. - `_calculate_item_transfer_qty` takes `pending_to_issue = required_qty − transferred_qty` and `desire_to_transfer = fg_completed_qty × required_qty / wo.qty`. - `_is_overproduction_allowed` allows more only within the extra-materials percentage, or the overproduction percentage if that is 0. - `_resolve_transfer_qty` decides the final quantity. If every item is already transferred, the code shows "All items have already been transferred for this Work Order." I didn't read `_resolve_transfer_qty`, so I haven't checked how it picks the final quantity.

What does this function do today (validations, merging/deduplication, round-off handling), and what would be affected by the change — enumerate the main document types and modules that call it.

final answer
`make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)` in `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py` (line 34) is the single entry point that turns a list of GL dicts into submitted GL Entry docs. It also handles cancellation. I read the file and grepped for callers, but I did not run anything. ## What it does today If `gl_map` is empty, nothing happens. Otherwise it takes one of two paths. **Non-cancel path** 1. **Budget check.** `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()` runs unless `use_legacy_budget_controller` is set or the voucher is a Period Closing Voucher. 2. **Dimension offsetting.** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` appends mirrored offsetting entries. This applies to accounting dimensions that have an automatic balancing account configured and whose values differ across the entries. 3. **Validations.** `validate_accounting_period` and `validate_disabled_accounts` run next. 4. **`process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)`**, which does three things: - For non-PCV vouchers, `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation` splits entries across cost centers according to Cost Center Allocation. It uses the company round-off account and default currency. - If `merge_entries` is true, `merge_similar_entries` runs. - `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` always runs. 5. **Payment Ledger.** If more than one entry remains and the voucher is not a PCV, `create_payment_ledger_entry` is called. It receives `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding` and `from_repost`. 6. **Saving.** `save_entries` is called. If only one entry remains, it throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found". **Cancel path** - It calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)`. `merge_entries` and `from_repost` are ignored here. ## Merging and deduplication **`merge_similar_entries`** - Entries with `_skip_merge` are left as they are. - All other entries get a `merge_key` built by `get_merge_key` from the fields listed in `get_merge_properties`: - account, cost_center, party, party_type, voucher_detail_no - against_voucher, against_voucher_type - project, finance_book, voucher_no - advance_voucher_type, advance_voucher_no - plus all accounting dimensions - Entries with the same key are summed in `check_if_in_list`. The summed fields are debit and credit, in company currency, account currency and transaction currency. - Entries where debit and credit are both zero at company-currency precision are dropped. The exception is a Journal Entry whose voucher_type is "Exchange Gain Or Loss", which is kept. **`toggle_debit_credit_if_negative`** - A negative debit becomes a credit, and a negative credit becomes a debit. - If both are equally negative, both are flipped to positive. - If `post_net_value` is set, debit and credit are netted against each other. ## Validations and round-off in `save_entries` - `validate_cwip_accounts` runs unless `from_repost`. - `process_debit_credit_difference` rounds debit and credit to the GL Entry precision and computes the difference. It does the same for the transaction-currency difference. - **Allowance.** The allowance is 5/10^precision for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for every other voucher type. - **Over the allowance.** The code raises "Debit and Credit not equal". Exchange Gain Or Loss Journal Entries are exempt. - **Within the allowance but at least one unit of precision.** `make_round_off_gle` runs. It takes the round-off account and cost center from `get_round_off_account_and_cost_center`, or the "Round Off for Opening" account when any entry is opening. It throws if that account is not set. - **Existing round-off row.** If the gl_map already has a row on that account, it is reused or removed when the difference cancels. For other voucher types, a new row is appended with no party or against-voucher. `update_accounting_dimensions` fills in its dimensions. - **After round-off.** The difference is re-checked. - Then `check_freezing_date` runs, using `adv_adj`. - `validate_against_pcv` runs for non-PCV vouchers. - For each entry, `validate_allowed_dimensions` runs. - `make_entry` then creates and submits a GL Entry. Its flags are `from_repost`, `adv_adj` and `update_outstanding`. After submit it calls `validate_expense_against_budget` unless the entry is from a repost or a PCV. ## What a change would affect **Callers that import the function and call it directly** - `sales_invoice.py` (`SalesInvoice.make_gl_entries`) passes `merge_entries=False`. - `purchase_invoice.py` (`PurchaseInvoice.make_gl_entries`) passes `merge_entries=False`. - `payment_entry.py` has two calls. One in `PaymentEntry.make_gl_entries` passes `merge_entries` from the Accounts Settings field `merge_similar_account_heads`, and it also calls `process_gl_map` itself first. The other is at line 1316 and passes `update_outstanding`. - `journal_entry.py` (`JournalEntry.make_gl_entries`) passes `merge_entries` from the same setting. - `asset_capitalization.py` passes `merge_entries=False, from_repost`. - `asset.py` and `asset_repair.py` call it positionally. - `invoice_discounting.py` passes `update_outstanding="No"`. - `period_closing_voucher.py` and `process_period_closing_voucher.py` pass `merge_entries=False`. - `process_deferred_accounting.py` passes `gl_map=` as a keyword and `cancel=1`. - `accounts/deferred_revenue.py` has its own wrapper, also named `make_gl_entries`, which calls the core function with `merge_entries=True`. - `stock_controller.py` (`StockController.make_gl_entries`) calls it with `from_repost=from_repost`. **Doctypes that inherit the stock controller** - These get the effect through `StockController.make_gl_entries`: - Purchase Receipt - Delivery Note - Stock Entry - Stock Reconciliation - Subcontracting Receipt - Landed Cost Voucher, which calls `doc.make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` on the affected receipts **Indirect callers through the doctype methods** - `repost_accounting_ledger.py` calls `doc.make_gl_entries(...)` for many voucher types. It passes `from_repost`, `cancel` or both. - `accounts/utils.py` (around line 1705) calls `voucher_obj.make_gl_entries(gl_entries=..., from_repost=True)`. Repost Item Valuation reaches this code. - `controllers/ledger_preview.py` calls `doc.make_gl_entries()`. - Patches call `doc.make_gl_entries()`: - v12 `repost_stock_ledger_entries_for_target_warehouse` - v13 `modify_invalid_gain_loss_gl_entries` - v15 `repost_gl_entries_with_no_account_subcontracting` **Related functions in the same file that share its logic** - `process_gl_map` is also called directly by Payment Entry and others. - `merge_similar_entries` and `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` are used from there. - `make_reverse_gl_entries` is used by cancellation in Sales Invoice, Asset Capitalization and elsewhere. - `save_entries` and `process_debit_credit_difference` hold the round-off logic. ## Practical risks - Most callers pass the keyword arguments `cancel`, `merge_entries`, `update_outstanding`, `adv_adj` and `from_repost`. Keep those names stable, or keep compatibility shims. The exceptions are `asset_repair.py`, which passes `cancel` positionally, and `asset.py`, which passes only `gl_entries`. - Changing the merge behavior affects the Accounts Settings field `merge_similar_account_heads` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry. It also affects the voucher types that call with `merge_entries=False`, where the `_skip_merge` flag and the `merge_key` fields matter. - Changing which fields go into the merge key changes the GL rows that Payment Ledger entries are built from, because `create_payment_ledger_entry` runs after merge. - Merge changes also affect the zero-entry filter and the Exchange Gain Or Loss exception. - Changes to round-off also affect the PCV and opening-entry logic.

final answer
`make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)` is at `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py:34-74`. It does nothing if `gl_map` is empty. I read the function and the helpers it calls. I didn't read every call site; I found callers by grepping imports. ## What it does today **Cancel path (`cancel=True`)** - It calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)`. - No validation or merging runs on this path. **Submit path (`cancel=False`), in order** 1. **Budget check:** - It runs `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()`. - It is skipped when `use_legacy_budget_controller` is set in Accounts Settings. - It is also skipped for Period Closing Voucher. 2. **Dimension offsetting:** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` mutates `gl_map` in place. - It applies when an Accounting Dimension has "automatically post balancing entry" and its values differ across the entries. - It appends mirrored debit/credit rows posted to the dimension's offsetting account. - Those rows have party and against_voucher cleared. 3. **Validators:** - `validate_accounting_period` and `validate_disabled_accounts` (both in `accounts/services/gl_validator.py`). 4. **`process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)`**, which does three things: - `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation` runs for everything except Period Closing Voucher. - It splits entries by Cost Center Allocation percentages, rounded to the GL Entry precision. - It validates the budget against the main cost center unless `from_repost`. - It leaves the round-off account row unsplit and moves it to the first allocated cost center. - `merge_similar_entries` runs only if `merge_entries` is true. - Rows with `_skip_merge` are kept as they are. - The merge key (`get_merge_properties`) is: account, cost_center, party, party_type, voucher_detail_no, against_voucher, against_voucher_type, project, finance_book, voucher_no, advance_voucher_type, advance_voucher_no, plus all accounting dimensions. - Matching rows have their debit and credit summed in company currency, account currency and transaction currency. - Rows that net to zero at GL precision are dropped. - The exception is a Journal Entry of type "Exchange Gain Or Loss", whose zero rows are kept. - `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` flips negative amounts to the opposite side, and nets debit against credit when `post_net_value` is set. 5. **Posting:** - With more than one entry and a voucher type other than Period Closing Voucher, it calls `create_payment_ledger_entry` (in `utils.py`) first. - It then calls `save_entries` for every voucher type with more than one entry. - With exactly one entry left after processing, it throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries…". **`save_entries` and round-off** - Unless `from_repost`, it runs `validate_cwip_accounts`. - It then calls `process_debit_credit_difference`: - It rounds debit and credit to GL precision and computes the difference. - The allowance is 5 / 10^precision for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for other voucher types. - A difference above the allowance raises "Debit and Credit not equal…", except for Journal Entries of type Exchange Gain Or Loss. - A difference below the allowance but at least one unit of precision calls `make_round_off_gle`. - It re-checks the difference after the round-off row is added. - `make_round_off_gle` takes the account from the Company's `round_off_account`, falling back to `default_expense_account`. If any entry is opening (`is_opening == "Yes"`), it uses `round_off_for_opening` instead and throws if that isn't set. - The cost center is the voucher's own cost center if it has one, otherwise `round_off_cost_center`. - If a round-off row already exists, it is adjusted, and removed if the remainder is below one unit of precision. - Otherwise a new row is appended with party and against_voucher cleared. - It sets accounting dimensions through `update_accounting_dimensions`. - `save_entries` also runs `check_freezing_date`, `validate_against_pcv` (skipped for Period Closing Voucher), and `validate_allowed_dimensions`. - For each entry, `make_entry` creates and submits a `GL Entry`. It also runs `validate_expense_against_budget` unless `from_repost` or the voucher is a Period Closing Voucher. - `make_entry` sets `gle.flags` from `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding` and `from_repost`. These flags are consumed in `GL Entry`. ## What a change would affect **Direct callers of the module function** - Repost: `repost_accounting_ledger.start_repost`, `accounts/utils.py::repost_gle_for_stock_vouchers`, which uses `from_repost`. Stock and Repost Item Valuation reposting reach it this way. - `controllers/ledger_preview.py::get_accounting_ledger_preview`. It runs the same path for the ledger preview, so any new validation or merge side effect shows up there. - `process_period_closing_voucher.summarize_and_post_ledger_entries`, and `period_closing_voucher.py:470`. Period Closing Voucher is special-cased in several places above. **Importers of the function** - Sales Invoice (`sales_invoice.py:1031`, also imports `make_reverse_gl_entries`). - Purchase Invoice. - Payment Entry. - Journal Entry (`journal_entry.py:867`). - `controllers/stock_controller.py`, which covers Stock Entry, Purchase Receipt, Delivery Note, Stock Reconciliation and Subcontracting Receipt through their `make_gl_entries` methods. - Asset (`asset.py:979`), Asset Capitalization (also imports `make_reverse_gl_entries`), and Asset Repair. - `accounts/deferred_revenue.py` and Process Deferred Accounting. - Invoice Discounting. **Indirect, through other `make_gl_entries` methods** - `accounts_controller` and the Landed Cost Voucher, POS Invoice and Opening Invoice flows go through the document-level `make_gl_entries` methods. - Those methods share the name but are different functions. The call graph tool resolved only 4 callers because of this name clash. Grep your own call sites rather than trusting a name search. **Related functions affected by a merge change** - `process_gl_map` is also called directly, for example for previews and in stock code. - `merge_similar_entries`, `get_merge_properties`, `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative`, `save_entries`, `process_debit_credit_difference` and `make_round_off_gle` are all in the same file. - Tests that assert on GL rows will also be affected. **Practical advice** - Keep the new parameters as defaults after the existing ones. Most callers pass the existing ones as keywords. - Changing merge behavior alters the GL Entry rows, the Payment Ledger Entries, and outstanding amounts.

final answer
## What `make_gl_entries` does today It is defined at `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py:34`. Its signature is `(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)`. **Flow when `cancel=False`** 1. **Budget validation.** If the legacy budget controller is off and the voucher is not a Period Closing Voucher, it runs `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()`. 2. **`make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry`.** This adds offsetting entries for accounting dimensions. 3. **`validate_accounting_period`.** 4. **`validate_disabled_accounts`.** 5. **`process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)`**, which does three things: - For non-Period Closing Vouchers it calls `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation`. - If `merge_entries` is true, it calls `merge_similar_entries`. - It always calls `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative`. 6. **Posting.** If more than one entry remains: - For non-Period Closing Vouchers it calls `create_payment_ledger_entry` (this carries `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding` and `from_repost`). - It then calls `save_entries`. 7. **Too few entries.** If exactly one entry remains, it throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found…". If the list is empty after processing, it does nothing. **Cancel path.** If `cancel=True`, it only calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)`. The `merge_entries` and `from_repost` arguments are ignored on this path. **Merging and deduplication** - **`merge_similar_entries`:** - Entries with `_skip_merge` set are kept as they are. - Every other entry gets a merge key from `get_merge_properties`: account, cost_center, party, party_type, voucher_detail_no, against_voucher, against_voucher_type, project, finance_book, voucher_no, advance_voucher_type, advance_voucher_no, plus all accounting dimensions. - Entries with the same key are combined by summing debit, credit, and the `_in_account_currency` and `_in_transaction_currency` variants. - Entries whose debit and credit are both zero at company precision are dropped. The exception is entries of a Journal Entry whose type is "Exchange Gain Or Loss". - **`toggle_debit_credit_if_negative`:** a negative debit moves to the credit side, and a negative credit moves to the debit side. This is done per currency field. If `post_net_value` is set, debit and credit are netted against each other. **Validations and round-off, inside `save_entries`** - `validate_cwip_accounts` runs unless `from_repost` is set. - `process_debit_credit_difference` rounds debit and credit to company precision and computes the debit−credit difference. It does the same for transaction currency. - The allowance is `5/10^precision` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for every other voucher type. - If the difference exceeds the allowance, it raises "Debit and Credit not equal…". Journal Entries of type "Exchange Gain Or Loss" are exempt. - If the difference is at least one unit of precision but within the allowance, `make_round_off_gle` appends a round-off GL entry. The account and cost center come from `get_round_off_account_and_cost_center`, and `update_accounting_dimensions` fills in its dimensions. - It then re-checks the difference after the round-off entry is added. - `check_freezing_date` runs, with `adv_adj` taken into account. - `validate_against_pcv` runs for non-Period Closing Vouchers and takes `is_opening` into account. - For each entry, `validate_allowed_dimensions` runs, then `make_entry` creates and submits the GL Entry document. - `make_entry` sets the flags `from_repost`, `adv_adj` and `update_outstanding`. - It then runs `validate_expense_against_budget` unless the entry is from a repost or belongs to a Period Closing Voucher. ## What a change would affect **Direct callers of `make_gl_entries`.** The graph shows these: - **Sales Invoice.** `sales_invoice.py` has `SalesInvoice.make_gl_entries`, and its `services/gl_composer.py` also uses `merge_similar_entries`. - **Purchase Invoice.** `purchase_invoice.py` has `PurchaseInvoice.make_gl_entries`, and its `services/gl_composer.py` also uses `merge_similar_entries`. - **Payment Entry.** `PaymentEntry.make_gl_entries`. - **Journal Entry.** `JournalEntry.make_gl_entries`. - **Invoice Discounting.** - **Period Closing Voucher.** `period_closing_voucher.py` (`process_gl_and_closing_entries`) and `process_period_closing_voucher.py` (`summarize_and_post_ledger_entries`). - **Deferred accounting.** - `deferred_revenue.py` (`book_deferred_income_or_expense` and a local `make_gl_entries`). - `process_deferred_accounting.on_cancel`. - **Assets.** Asset, Asset Capitalization and Asset Repair. - **Stock.** `StockController.make_gl_entries` in `controllers/stock_controller.py`. Stock-based documents such as Purchase Receipt, Delivery Note, Stock Entry, Stock Reconciliation and Subcontracting Receipt reach the function through this controller. This is inferred from the shared `StockController`, not confirmed document by document. **Other functions in the same module.** Several modules call helper functions in `general_ledger.py` rather than `make_gl_entries` itself. A change to merge behavior would reach them because they share the helpers: - `process_gl_map` is called from the `gl_composer.py` modules for Stock Entry, Purchase Receipt and Subcontracting Receipt, and from `stock/services/base_stock_gl_composer.py`. - `merge_similar_entries` is called from the Sales Invoice and Purchase Invoice `gl_composer.py` files. - `get_round_off_account_and_cost_center` is used by `accounts_controller.py` and the Sales Invoice and Purchase Invoice composers. - `process_debit_credit_difference` and `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` are used in `accounts/utils.py`. - `make_reverse_gl_entries` is used by Repost Accounting Ledger, Asset, Asset Capitalization, Sales Invoice, Period Closing Voucher and the `single_to_multi_dunning` patch. **Things to watch if you change it** - Callers that pass `merge_entries`, `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding` or `from_repost` by keyword or position. Keep the existing order or update every call site. - Tests under `**/test_*` were excluded from my search, so they will also need updating. - Repost flows, which rely on `from_repost` and on `create_payment_ledger_entry`. - The `_skip_merge` flag and the merge-key fields that composers depend on. - The round-off allowance, which differs for Journal Entry and Payment Entry.

final answer
`make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)` is in `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py:34`. I read the code directly and did not run it or its tests. The caller list comes from grep over `make_gl_entries(` and the `general_ledger` imports, so it is only as complete as those patterns. ## What it does today If `gl_map` is empty, it returns without doing anything. **Validations (only when `cancel=False`):** - **Budget:** `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()` runs unless the `use_legacy_budget_controller` setting is on or the voucher is a Period Closing Voucher. - **Dimension offsetting:** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` appends offsetting GL entries for accounting dimensions that have an automatic balancing account. It does this in place, by mutating the list. - **Accounting period:** `validate_accounting_period`. - **Disabled accounts:** `validate_disabled_accounts`. - **Entry count:** after processing, a result with exactly one entry throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found". **Processing:** `process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)` does three things in order: 1. **Cost center allocation:** it runs `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation`, except for Period Closing Voucher. - A cost center with a Cost Center Allocation is split across the sub cost centers by percentage. - Budget is validated against the main cost center (skipped when `from_repost`). - The round-off account is moved to the main cost center and not split. 2. **Merge:** it runs `merge_similar_entries` when `merge_entries=True`. 3. **Negative amounts:** it runs `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative`. **Merging and deduplication (`merge_similar_entries`):** - Entries flagged `_skip_merge` are never merged. - The merge key is `get_merge_key` over these fields: account, cost_center, party, party_type, voucher_detail_no, against_voucher, against_voucher_type, project, finance_book, voucher_no, advance_voucher_type, advance_voucher_no, plus every accounting dimension. - Entries with the same key have their debit, credit, and the account-currency and transaction-currency variants summed. Matching is a linear scan, `check_if_in_list`. - After merging, entries that are zero at GL Entry precision are dropped. The exception is a Journal Entry whose `voucher_type` is "Exchange Gain Or Loss". **Negative amounts (`toggle_debit_credit_if_negative`):** this runs for the base, account-currency and transaction-currency field pairs. - A negative debit is moved to credit, and a negative credit is moved to debit. - If `post_net_value` is set and both sides are non-zero, they are netted into one side. **Persisting:** - `create_payment_ledger_entry` runs first, for every voucher type except Period Closing Voucher. - `save_entries` then does the rest: - It calls `validate_cwip_accounts`, skipped when `from_repost`. - It calls `process_debit_credit_difference`, which handles round-off (below). - It checks the freezing date and `validate_against_pcv`, which rejects postings into closed books. This check is skipped for Period Closing Voucher. - It calls `validate_allowed_dimensions` for each entry, then `make_entry`, which creates and submits a `GL Entry`. **Cancel path:** with `cancel=True`, none of the above runs. It calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)` instead. **Round-off (`process_debit_credit_difference`, `make_round_off_gle`):** - Debits and credits are first rounded to GL Entry precision. - The allowed difference is `5/10^precision` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for all other voucher types. - A difference above the allowance raises "Debit and Credit not equal". The exception is a Journal Entry of type Exchange Gain Or Loss. - A difference between one currency unit at that precision and the allowance gets a round-off entry from `make_round_off_gle`: - The account comes from the Company round-off settings. It is "Round Off for Opening" if any entry has `is_opening == "Yes"`, and a missing opening account throws. - An existing round-off entry is reused and adjusted, and removed if the difference becomes zero. - Otherwise a new entry is appended, with the party and against-voucher fields cleared and the accounting dimensions filled in. - The difference is recomputed afterwards and the same error is raised if it is still too large. ## What a change would affect **Direct callers of `make_gl_entries`:** - **Controllers:** - `StockController.make_gl_entries` in `controllers/stock_controller.py`, which covers Purchase Receipt, Delivery Note, Stock Entry, Stock Reconciliation, Subcontracting Receipt and Landed Cost Voucher. - `controllers/ledger_preview.py`. - **Accounts documents:** - Sales Invoice, Purchase Invoice, Payment Entry, Journal Entry. - Invoice Discounting, Period Closing Voucher, Process Period Closing Voucher. - Repost Accounting Ledger, Process Deferred Accounting. - **Other accounts modules:** `accounts/deferred_revenue.py` (which also defines its own differently-shaped `make_gl_entries`) and `accounts/utils.py` (stock GL reposting). - **Assets:** Asset (`asset.py`, `mapper.py`), Asset Repair, Asset Capitalization. - **Patches:** - `v15_0/repost_gl_entries_with_no_account_subcontracting.py` - `v13_0/modify_invalid_gain_loss_gl_entries.py` - `v12_0/repost_stock_ledger_entries_for_target_warehouse.py` - `v14_0/single_to_multi_dunning.py` (it matched the import grep, not the call grep) **Other code that depends on the merge and round-off helpers:** - `process_gl_map` is also used by other posting paths, so a change to merge behavior inside `process_gl_map` or `merge_similar_entries` reaches them too. I did not enumerate those users. - `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` is imported by `repost_gle_for_stock_vouchers` in `accounts/utils.py`. - `process_debit_credit_difference` is also called from `accounts/utils.py`. - `make_round_off_gle` is called only from `process_debit_credit_difference`. **Risks:** - Callers pass `cancel`, `merge_entries`, `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding` and `from_repost` as keyword or positional arguments, so any signature change has to be checked at every call site above. - Merge changes affect the payment ledger entries, because `create_payment_ledger_entry` runs on the processed map. - They also affect round-off, because the merged map is what gets balanced. - They affect repost flows and per-voucher tests, which depend on the number of entries and on the `_skip_merge` and Exchange Gain Or Loss special cases.

final answer
No, the report doesn't break. When the detail query for a group value finds no data, the report skips that row and carries on. The logic is in `get_data()` in `erpnext/controllers/trends.py`, in the `if filters.get("group_by"):` branch (lines 107–222). It runs in three steps: 1. The first query (`data1`) returns the aggregated rows for the selected "Based On" dimension. Each one gets a blank placeholder inserted at the group-by column index (`dt.insert(ind, "")`) and is appended to `data`. 2. For each based-on row, a second query fetches the `DISTINCT` group-by values (`sel_col`, such as `t2.item_code`, `t1.customer` or `t1.supplier`). It filters on `based_on_key = data1[d][0]`. 3. For each distinct group value, a third query (`row1`) fetches the period-wise sums. It is grouped by `t4.default_currency` and `sel_col`. The third query can come back empty. In that case the code hits this check (lines 210–211) and moves on to the next group value: ```python if not row1: continue ``` Without the guard, the later `row1[0][0]` and `row1[0][j]` lookups would raise an `IndexError`. With it, no detail row is added for that group value. The based-on summary row stays, and the remaining group values and the final total row (`calculate_total_row`) are built as usual. If the detail query finds nothing, the report just has no breakdown row under that based-on row. I read the code but didn't run the report.

Q08Group B — Caller/callee trace + impact analysisWhen ERPNext checks a Material Request or Purchase Order against a Budget, how does the system compute the "requested amount" acro…
When ERPNext checks a Material Request or Purchase Order against a Budget, how does the system compute the "requested amount" across multiple matching line items, and why does the placement of rate relative to the aggregate Sum() function matter for correctness?

Baseline (no tools) 83.3%

turns: 2
tool calls: 1
cost: $0.0272
elapsed: 14.0s
inputoutputcache createcache readtotal
41,1031,86912,01414,990
Grep×1
  1. #1Grep
    {"pattern": "def get_requested_amount|def get_ordered_amount", "path": "/benchmarks/dataset/repos/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py", "output_mode": "content", "-A": 35}
    → 689:def get_requested_amount(params): 690- item_code = params.get("item_code") 691- 692- child = frappe.qb.DocType("Material Request Item") 693- parent = frappe.qb.DocType("Material Request") 694- 695- data = ( 696- frappe.qb.from_(child) 697- .join(parent) 698- .on(parent.name == child.parent) 6…
final answer
**How it's computed** The calculation is in `get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`. It queries `Material Request Item` joined to its parent `Material Request`. - **Row filter:** a row is included only if all of these hold: - `item_code` matches. - The parent has `docstatus == 1`. - `material_request_type == "Purchase"`. - The parent status is not "Stopped". - `stock_qty > ordered_qty`, so only lines with quantity still unordered count. - `get_other_condition` matches the expense account, the budget-against dimension (cost center or project), and the fiscal-year date range, using `schedule_date` for Material Requests. - **Per-row value:** each matching row contributes `(stock_qty - ordered_qty) * rate`. That is the pending quantity times the rate. - **Aggregate:** the query selects `Coalesce(Sum((child.stock_qty - child.ordered_qty) * child.rate), 0)`. This adds up the pending value of every matching line and returns 0 if nothing matches. The function returns `data[0][0]`. `get_ordered_amount` does the same for Purchase Orders. It sums `Purchase Order Item.amount - billed_amt` for submitted, non-Closed orders where `amount > billed_amt`. It uses the same `get_other_condition` filter, with `transaction_date` as the date field. **Why rate must be inside `Sum()`** - **Correct result:** the amount is a per-line product, so it has to be `Sum(qty_pending * rate)`. Each line is multiplied by its own rate before the lines are added. - A form like `Sum(qty_pending) * rate` would multiply the total quantity by a single rate. Which rate that is depends on the database, because the rate column is not aggregated. - If lines for the same item have different rates, that gives the wrong total. For example, 10 units at 5 and 10 units at 7 should come to 120, not 20 times one of the two rates. - **PostgreSQL validity:** the code comment says that putting rate inside the aggregate is "PG-valid". PostgreSQL rejects a bare non-aggregated column in the select list unless it is in a `GROUP BY`. MariaDB/MySQL would accept `Sum(qty) * rate` and silently pick an arbitrary row's rate, which gives a wrong budget figure. With rate inside `Sum()`, every column in the expression is aggregated, so the query is valid on both databases and gives the right number. I read this from the code and its comment. I didn't run the query or check git history for an earlier version that had the rate outside the `Sum()`.

final answer
The requested amount is the sum of (stock_qty − ordered_qty) × rate over every matching Material Request Item row. The rate has to sit inside `Sum()` for that to come out right. **Where the calculation lives** There are two implementations: - **`get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`.** `compare_expense_with_budget` calls it, together with `get_ordered_amount`, whenever no explicit amount is passed. - **`BudgetValidation.get_requested_amount(key)` in `erpnext/controllers/budget_controller.py`.** It runs per `(dimension_type, dimension, account)` key and stores the result in `self.to_validate[key].requested_amount`. **How it is computed** 1. The query joins Material Request Item (`child`) to Material Request (`parent`). 2. It keeps only rows that meet these conditions: - The parent has `docstatus == 1`. - The parent has `material_request_type == "Purchase"`. - The parent's status is not "Stopped". - The row still has unordered quantity (`child.stock_qty > child.ordered_qty`). - The row's `item_code` matches (the controller version matches the document's item codes). - The row's `expense_account` matches. - The row's budget dimension matches (cost center or project). - The parent's date falls within the fiscal year. `get_other_condition` handles this, using `schedule_date` for a Material Request. 3. The select clause is `Coalesce(Sum((child.stock_qty - child.ordered_qty) * child.rate), 0)`. The controller version wraps each field in `IfNull(..., 0)` instead. 4. The result is a single scalar. The code returns `data[0][0]`, or 0 if there are no rows. 5. In `compare_expense_with_budget`, a Material Request is checked against `requested_amount + ordered_amount`. A Purchase Order is checked against `ordered_amount`. That figure is added to `actual_expense` and compared with the budget. **Why the rate placement matters** Each line has its own rate, so the amount has to be worked out per row and then summed: Σ((stock_qty − ordered_qty) × rate). The comment in the code says the same thing: "rate inside the aggregate: Sum(qty * rate) is the correct requested amount and is PG-valid". - **Rate outside the aggregate** would look like `Sum(stock_qty − ordered_qty) * rate`. That multiplies the total pending quantity by one rate, and it is wrong in two ways: - **Wrong number:** items with different rates get priced at a single rate. - **Invalid on PostgreSQL:** a bare, non-aggregated `rate` column next to an aggregate needs a `GROUP BY`. MySQL/MariaDB tolerate that and return an arbitrary row's rate, but PostgreSQL rejects the query. - **Rate inside `Sum()`** applies each row's own rate before aggregating. The query then needs no `GROUP BY` and is valid on both database types. The `Coalesce`/`IfNull` wrappers turn an empty result or NULL fields into 0, so the budget comparison never receives `None`. I didn't open `get_ordered_amount` or `BudgetValidation.validate_for_overbooking`, so I haven't checked whether they use the same pattern.

final answer
When ERPNext checks a Material Request or Purchase Order against a Budget, it adds up the unordered value of every matching Material Request line in a single SQL aggregate. Each line is valued as (quantity not yet ordered) × (that line's own rate). The rate has to be inside `Sum()`, because the rate differs from line to line. **Where the code is** There are two implementations: - **`BudgetController.get_requested_amount(self, key)`** in `erpnext/controllers/budget_controller.py` (about line 239) is the main one. The controller's budget validation calls it. - **`get_requested_amount(params)`** in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` (about line 689) is a module-level helper with the same logic. **How the amount is computed** 1. **Which lines match.** The query joins `Material Request` to `Material Request Item` and keeps only lines that meet all of these: - The Material Request is submitted (`docstatus = 1`), has type `Purchase`, and its status is not `Stopped`. - The company matches, and `transaction_date` falls within the fiscal year (`fy_start_date` to `fy_end_date`). - The line's `expense_account` matches the budget key's GL account, and the budget dimension field matches too. The key is `(dimension_type, dimension, account)`. - When the document being checked is a Purchase Order or Material Request, the line's `item_code` must be one of the items on that document. - In the `budget.py` version, `stock_qty > ordered_qty`, so fully ordered lines drop out. 2. **How the lines are summed.** The controller version runs one aggregate over all matching lines: `Sum((IfNull(stock_qty,0) - IfNull(ordered_qty,0)) * IfNull(rate,0)).as_("amount")`. `budget.py` has the same expression wrapped in `Coalesce(..., 0)`. 3. **What is stored.** The result is saved as `self.to_validate[key].requested_amount`, and it is 0 if no lines match. The budget check then compares this value, along with the ordered amount and actual expense, against the budget. **Why the position of rate matters** - **Inside `Sum()` (correct).** Each line contributes its own `(stock_qty − ordered_qty) × rate`, and these products are added up. The result is the true outstanding monetary value. For example, one line with 10 units at rate 5 and another with 2 units at rate 100 gives 10×5 + 2×100 = 250. - **Outside `Sum()` (incorrect).** An expression like `Sum(qty − ordered) * rate` is wrong in two ways: - **Invalid on PostgreSQL.** `rate` is a plain column, not an aggregate and not in a `GROUP BY`, so Postgres rejects the query. The comment in `budget.py` says exactly this: "rate inside the aggregate: Sum(qty * rate) is the correct requested amount and is PG-valid". - **Wrong on MariaDB.** MariaDB would accept it, but it would pick an arbitrary row's rate and multiply the total remaining quantity by it. That misvalues any set of lines with different rates, so the budget would be over- or under-stated. The `IfNull` and `Coalesce` wrappers stop NULL quantities or rates from turning the whole sum into NULL.

final answer
ERPNext computes the requested amount in SQL. It sums, per line, the unordered quantity multiplied by that line's rate. The rate has to sit inside `Sum()` so it is applied to each row before the rows are added together. **Where it is computed** There are two implementations. Both query Material Request Item joined to its parent Material Request. 1. `get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` (line 689) is the older path, called from `compare_expense_with_budget`. - It selects `Coalesce(Sum((child.stock_qty - child.ordered_qty) * child.rate), 0)`. - It filters on: - `item_code`; - `parent.docstatus == 1`; - `stock_qty > ordered_qty`, so only lines with something still unordered count; - `material_request_type == "Purchase"`; - `status != "Stopped"`; - the extra dimension, account and date conditions from `get_other_condition`. - A comment in the code says it directly: "rate inside the aggregate: Sum(qty * rate) is the correct requested amount and is PG-valid". 2. `BudgetValidation.get_requested_amount(key)` in `erpnext/controllers/budget_controller.py` (line 239) is the newer path, used by `validate_for_overbooking`. - It is keyed by `(dimension, value, expense_account)`. - It computes `Sum((IfNull(mri.stock_qty,0) - IfNull(mri.ordered_qty,0)) * IfNull(mri.rate,0))`. - It restricts the query to: - submitted, non-stopped Purchase-type Material Requests in the fiscal year; - the matching `expense_account`; - the matching dimension value; - the item codes on the current document. - The result goes into `to_validate[key].requested_amount`. - `handle_material_request_overlimit` then checks `requested_amount + current_requested_amount` against the annual budget and the accumulated monthly budget. - The `ordered_amount` for Purchase Orders is computed the same way, using `Sum(poi.amount - poi.billed_amt)`. **Why the placement of rate matters** Every matching Material Request line has its own rate, so the correct total is Σ(qtyᵢ × rateᵢ). The code does this by multiplying inside the aggregate. A common mistake is to write `Sum(qty - ordered_qty) * rate`. That multiplies the summed quantity by a single `rate` that is outside the aggregate. This is wrong in two ways: - **Wrong total.** With several lines at different rates, the formula gives (Σqty) × rate, where rate is whichever one value is picked. For example, 10 units at rate 5 plus 10 units at rate 100 should give 1050. Multiplying the summed quantity by one rate gives 100 or 2000, depending on which rate is used. That understates or overstates the budget consumed, so budget checks can pass or fail wrongly. - **Invalid SQL on PostgreSQL.** A bare `rate` outside the aggregate is not in a `GROUP BY` and not inside an aggregate. PostgreSQL rejects that query. MariaDB/MySQL accepts it and quietly returns an arbitrary row's rate. The code comment's "PG-valid" refers to this. Putting the multiplication inside `Sum()` fixes both problems. `Coalesce` and `IfNull` guard against NULL quantities or rates, and `Coalesce(..., 0)` gives 0 when no rows match.

final answer
**How `POSInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments` repopulates the payments table** The method is in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py` (line ~796). It is whitelisted. The Sales Invoice version in `sales_invoice/services/pos.py` (line ~173) does the same thing. 1. If `self.pos_profile` is set, it loads the profile with `frappe.get_cached_doc("POS Profile", self.pos_profile)`. 2. It calls the shared helper `update_multi_mode_option(self, pos_profile)`. 3. It sets `self.paid_amount = 0`, so the cashier has to re-enter the amounts. **What `update_multi_mode_option` does** (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/pos.py`, line ~339) 1. It clears the table with `doc.set("payments", [])`. 2. It collects the `mode_of_payment` values from the profile's `payments` rows. 3. It calls `get_mode_of_payments_info(mode_of_payments, doc.company)`. That runs a query through `_enabled_mode_of_payment_query`, which joins `Mode of Payment Account` to `Mode of Payment`. The query keeps only enabled modes for the doc's company. It returns a dict keyed by mode name, holding `default_account` and `type`. 4. For each profile row: - If no info is found, the mode is added to an `invalid_modes` list and skipped. - Otherwise it copies the row's `default` flag onto the info, and the inner `append_payment` adds a payments row. That row gets `default`, `mode_of_payment`, `account` (the default account) and `type`. 5. If any modes were invalid, it throws a "Missing Account" error that links to each one. Its singular/plural check compares the list itself to 1 (`invalid_modes == 1`) instead of using `len()`, so the singular message is never chosen. 6. If the table was refreshed, it shows an orange toast: "Payment methods refreshed. Please review before proceeding." **How it avoids breaking on a doctype without Sales-Invoice-only fields** The refresh flag is computed as `mop_refetched = bool(doc.payments) and not doc.get("is_created_using_pos")`. `is_created_using_pos` exists on Sales Invoice but not on POS Invoice. Plain attribute access on a POS Invoice would raise `AttributeError`. `doc.get("is_created_using_pos")` returns `None` instead, so the check works on both doctypes. This is the reason the code comment says to use `get()`. On a POS Invoice that already has payments, `mop_refetched` is true, so the "refreshed" toast appears.

final answer
When a cashier reopens a saved draft POS Invoice, `POSInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments` rebuilds the payments table from the POS Profile. It does this by calling a shared helper, `update_multi_mode_option`. I read the code and did not run it. **`POSInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments`** (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py`, around line 796) - It only acts if `self.pos_profile` is set. - It loads the profile with `frappe.get_cached_doc("POS Profile", self.pos_profile)`. - It passes the invoice and the profile to `update_multi_mode_option(self, pos_profile)`. - It then sets `self.paid_amount = 0`. - `SalesInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments` is a thin wrapper that calls `POSService(self).reset_mode_of_payments()`. That is the Sales Invoice path. **`update_multi_mode_option`** (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/pos.py`, lines 339-376) 1. It clears the table with `doc.set("payments", [])`. 2. It collects the `mode_of_payment` names from `pos_profile.payments`. It then calls `get_mode_of_payments_info(modes, doc.company)` to get each mode's default account and type for that company. 3. It loops over the profile's payment rows. Each row is handled in one of two ways: - If the mode has no info, its Mode of Payment link is added to `invalid_modes` and the loop moves on. - Otherwise it copies the profile row's `default` flag onto the mode info. The inner `append_payment` then adds a row to `doc.payments` with `default`, `mode_of_payment` (taken from `mop`), `account` (taken from `default_account`) and `type`. 4. If any modes were invalid, it calls `frappe.throw` with a "Missing Account" message ("Please set default Cash or Bank account in Mode of Payment(s) …"). The check that chooses the singular or plural wording is `invalid_modes == 1`. That compares a list to 1, so it is never true and the plural message is always used. This looks like a bug, though it doesn't affect the rest of the flow. 5. If the table already had rows before the reset, it shows a toast: "Payment methods refreshed. Please review before proceeding." The toast is orange. **Handling a doctype that lacks Sales-Invoice-only fields** The helper is shared by Sales Invoice and POS Invoice. `is_created_using_pos` exists on Sales Invoice but not on POS Invoice. Reading `doc.is_created_using_pos` directly on a POS Invoice would raise `AttributeError`. So the helper computes the "refetched" flag as `bool(doc.payments) and not doc.get("is_created_using_pos")`. `doc.get` returns `None` when the field is missing, so the flag is true for a POS Invoice that already had payments, and nothing breaks. Other callers of the helper are `POSInvoice.set_pos_fields` and `POSService._apply_editable_pos_defaults`.

final answer
`reset_mode_of_payments` rebuilds the `payments` table from the POS Profile. The shared helper `update_multi_mode_option` does the work, and it reads the Sales-Invoice-only field with `doc.get(...)` so it doesn't fail on a POS Invoice. I read the code but didn't run it. **Entry points.** There are three versions of `reset_mode_of_payments`, and each does the same thing: - `POSInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py` (line 796) is whitelisted. - `SalesInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments` is in `sales_invoice.py` (line 723). - A `POSService.reset_mode_of_payments` is in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/pos.py` (line 173). **How the table is repopulated.** 1. If the document has a `pos_profile`, it loads that profile with `frappe.get_cached_doc("POS Profile", pos_profile)`. 2. It calls `update_multi_mode_option(doc, pos_profile)`. 3. It then sets `paid_amount = 0`. Inside `update_multi_mode_option` (`services/pos.py`, line 339): 1. It clears the table with `doc.set("payments", [])`. 2. It collects the mode names from `pos_profile.payments`. 3. It calls `get_mode_of_payments_info(modes, doc.company)`. This runs one query that joins the enabled `Mode of Payment` records to their `Mode of Payment Account` rows for the company. It returns a dict keyed by mode with `default_account`, `mop` and `type`. 4. For each profile row, it takes the matching info and sets `payment_mode.default = row.default` (the profile's default flag). The inner `append_payment` then adds a row with `default`, `mode_of_payment`, `account` (the mode's default account) and `type`. 5. A mode with no enabled account for the company is added to `invalid_modes`. After the loop, a "Missing Account" `frappe.throw` lists them. **Why it doesn't break on a POS Invoice.** The helper decides whether to show a "refreshed" notice with this line: `mop_refetched = bool(doc.payments) and not doc.get("is_created_using_pos")` `is_created_using_pos` exists on Sales Invoice but not on POS Invoice. Reading it as an attribute (`doc.is_created_using_pos`) would raise `AttributeError` on a POS Invoice. `doc.get()` returns `None` for a missing field instead. The check is also made before `payments` is cleared, so it records whether the table already had rows. If `mop_refetched` is true, the helper ends with `frappe.toast("Payment methods refreshed. Please review before proceeding.", indicator="orange")`. I also noticed a bug in the error message: `if invalid_modes == 1` compares a list to an integer, so it is never true. The singular wording is never used, and the plural message is always shown.

`reset_mode_of_payments` clears the draft's payments table and rebuilds it from the POS Profile's payment rows. The shared helper does this with `doc.get()` instead of attribute access, so it doesn't fail on a POS Invoice. I read the source and didn't run it. **Entry point** `POSInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments` is in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py` (line ~796) and is whitelisted. 1. It runs only if `self.pos_profile` is set. 2. It loads the profile with `frappe.get_cached_doc("POS Profile", self.pos_profile)`. 3. It calls `update_multi_mode_option(self, pos_profile)`. 4. It sets `self.paid_amount = 0`. `POSService.reset_mode_of_payments` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/pos.py` does the same for Sales Invoice. **How the table is repopulated** The work is in `update_multi_mode_option(doc, pos_profile)` (`services/pos.py`, line ~339): 1. It records whether the existing payments are being replaced: `mop_refetched = bool(doc.payments) and not doc.get("is_created_using_pos")`. 2. It empties the table with `doc.set("payments", [])`. 3. It collects `mode_of_payment` from each row of the profile's `payments` table. It passes those names and `doc.company` to `get_mode_of_payments_info`, which returns each mode's default account and type. 4. The helper adds a link to that Mode of Payment to `invalid_modes` and skips the row. - Otherwise it copies the profile row's `default` flag onto the info and calls the inner `append_payment`. That appends a payments row with `default`, `mode_of_payment` (from `mop`), `account` (from `default_account`) and `type`. 5. If `invalid_modes` is non-empty, it throws a "Missing Account" error listing those modes. I think the singular/plural check is a bug: `invalid_modes == 1` compares a list to an integer, so it is never true and the plural message is always used. 6. If `mop_refetched` is true, it shows an orange toast: "Payment methods refreshed. Please review before proceeding." **Why it doesn't break on a POS Invoice** `is_created_using_pos` exists on Sales Invoice but not on POS Invoice. The helper uses `doc.get("is_created_using_pos")`, which returns `None` for a missing field. Direct attribute access would raise `AttributeError`. On a POS Invoice the flag is always falsy, so `mop_refetched` is true whenever the draft already had payment rows. The toast therefore always appears when a saved draft's payments are reset. On a Sales Invoice created through POS (`is_created_using_pos` set), the toast is suppressed.

final answer
The current per-item rate calculation is `calculate_item_rate` in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`. That is the server-side controller (`calculate_taxes_and_totals`). A client-side counterpart in `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js` works differently and is covered below. I read both files, but I didn't run anything. ## Server-side flow (`erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`) 1. `_calculate()` calls `validate_conversion_rate()`, then `calculate_item_values()`, and after that the tax and total steps. 2. `calculate_item_values()` returns early if the document is consolidated or `discount_amount_applied` is set. Otherwise, for each item it does the following: - It calls `round_floats_in(item)`, then `calculate_item_rate(item)`. - It sets `net_rate = rate`. - It sets `amount = rate * qty`. For a return or debit note with zero qty, it uses `-rate` or `rate` instead. - It sets `net_amount = amount`. - It calls `_set_in_company_currency` for `price_list_rate`, `rate_with_margin`, `rate`, `net_rate`, `amount` and `net_amount`, which fills the `base_*` fields. 3. `calculate_item_rate(item)` is the core logic: - **No price list rate.** If `price_list_rate` is empty, it calls `remove_margin` and `remove_discount`, sets `rate_with_margin = 0`, and returns. `rate` is left as the user entered it. - **Pricing rules.** `has_pricing_rules` is true when `item.pricing_rules` is set and `doc.ignore_pricing_rule` is not. In that case it: - clears the margin with `remove_margin`; - loops over `get_applied_pricing_rules(item.pricing_rules)` and loads each `Pricing Rule`; - copies `margin_type` and `margin_rate_or_amount` from any rule that has them. A "Percentage" margin always applies. An "Amount" margin applies only when the rule's currency matches the document currency. - **Rate with margin.** `item.rate_with_margin = get_rate_with_margin(item)`: - With no margin type, it returns `price_list_rate`. - With "Percentage", it returns `price_list_rate * (1 + margin/100)`. - With "Amount", it returns `price_list_rate + margin_rate_or_amount`. - **Discount.** If `discount_percentage > 0`, then `discount_amount = rate_with_margin * discount_percentage / 100`. Otherwise an existing `discount_amount` is used as it is. - **Calculated rate.** `calculated_rate = rate_with_margin - discount_amount`, rounded to the precision of the `rate` field. ## Manual rate override The last part of `calculate_item_rate` compares the calculated rate with what is already in `item.rate`: - **Rate is empty, or pricing rules apply.** `item.rate = calculated_rate`. A manual rate is therefore overwritten when a pricing rule is applied. - **Rate equals the calculated rate.** Nothing changes. - **Rate differs and is above `price_list_rate`.** The user's rate is kept and the margin and discount are rebuilt around it: - `margin_type = "Amount"` - `margin_rate_or_amount = rate - price_list_rate` - `rate_with_margin = rate` - `remove_discount(item)` - **Rate differs and is at or below `price_list_rate`.** The user's rate is kept and treated as a discount: - `rate_with_margin = price_list_rate` - `discount_amount = price_list_rate - rate` - `discount_percentage = 0` - `remove_margin(item)` So a manual rate wins unless a pricing rule is applied, and the margin and discount fields are changed to explain it. ## Client-side counterpart (`erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js`) - `apply_pricing_rule_on_item(item)` takes `price_list_rate`, or `blanket_order_rate` for Sales Order and Quotation items. - It applies the margin, either Percentage or Amount, and stores the result in `rate_with_margin`. - It converts `discount_percentage` into `discount_amount`, subtracts that, and sets `rate` with `frappe.model.set_value`. - `transaction.js` calls it from the `price_list_rate`, `margin_rate_or_amount` and `margin_type` handlers. - The JS `calculate_item_values()` does not recompute the rate. It only uses the current `item.rate` to compute `net_rate`, `amount`, the base fields and `total_weight`. - A manual rate edit in the browser therefore just flows through to the amounts. The reconciliation of margin and discount happens on the server in `calculate_item_rate`.
