final answer
The system prevents duplicate pickup in three ways. All of it is in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/process_period_closing_voucher/process_period_closing_voucher.py`. **How it starts.** Submitting a Period Closing Voucher runs `PeriodClosingVoucher.on_submit`. Unless the legacy controller setting is on, this creates and submits a `Process Period Closing Voucher` (PPCV) document. Its `validate` fills two child tables, `normal_balances` and `z_opening_balances`, with one `Process Period Closing Voucher Detail` row per date and report type (Profit and Loss, Balance Sheet). Every row starts as `Queued`. The PPCV's `on_submit` then calls `start_pcv_processing`, which calls `initialize_parallel_threads`. **1. Row-level locking with `SKIP LOCKED`.** `initialize_parallel_threads` selects up to 4 `Queued` rows with `.limit(threads).for_update(skip_locked=True)`. `schedule_next_date` does the same with `limit(1)`. `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` locks the chosen rows. Any other transaction running the same query skips them, so it cannot select the same row at the same time. **2. A status claim before enqueueing.** After selecting a row, the code sets its status from `Queued` to `Running` with `frappe.db.set_value`. Only after that does it call `frappe.enqueue(process_individual_date, ..., enqueue_after_commit=True)`. - In `schedule_next_date`, the code commits right after the status change. The comments say this keeps the transaction short and avoids REPEATABLE READ concurrency errors. - Once the row is `Running`, later `Queued` queries no longer return it, even without the lock. - `initialize_parallel_threads` commits only after the whole loop, and `enqueue_after_commit=True` makes each job wait for that commit. **3. A guard inside the worker.** `process_individual_date` first reads the row's status and returns immediately if it isn't `Running`. This means a job for a row that was paused or cancelled, or that was already handled, does nothing. - `pause_pcv_processing` sets rows to `Paused`. - `cancel_pcv_processing` sets `Queued` rows to `Cancelled`. **Chaining.** When a worker finishes, it sets the row to `Completed` and commits. It then chains to `schedule_next_date`, which claims the next `Queued` row through the same lock-and-claim steps. When no `Queued` rows remain, the PPCV is marked `Completed`, and `summarize_and_post_ledger_entries` posts the GL entries. I didn't read the final part of `process_individual_date` (after the commit) or the rest of `schedule_next_date`. The chaining and completion steps above come from the code I did read plus the call graph.

final answer
**How it decides.** The Job Card's `make_stock_entry` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/job_card/mapper.py` builds a "Material Transfer for Manufacture" Stock Entry. In its `set_missing_values`, it calls `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(target, item)` on every item row. That function and its helpers are in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/manufacturing.py`. 1. **Early exits.** `set_previous_operation_serial_batch` does nothing in these cases: - The Stock Entry has no `work_order`. - The row has no `s_warehouse`. - The quantity is 0 or less. The quantity used is `qty × conversion_factor`, so it is in stock UOM. - The row already has a `serial_and_batch_bundle`, `serial_no` or `batch_no`. 2. **Which items qualify.** `get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch(work_order, item_code, s_warehouse)` returns an empty pool unless two things hold: - The item is the `finished_good` of some Work Order Operation row of that Work Order. Ordinary raw materials therefore never qualify. - The item has `has_serial_no` or `has_batch_no`. 3. **Building the pool.** It takes what earlier operations produced and subtracts what has already been consumed. `_get_operation_bundles` and `_get_operation_sn_batch` do the lookups. - **Produced:** serial and batch entries from submitted Stock Entries for the same Work Order. These are "Manufacture" entries with `is_finished_item=1`, where `t_warehouse` equals the row's source warehouse. - **Consumed:** entries from submitted Stock Entries for the same Work Order where `s_warehouse` equals that warehouse. Cancelled entries are ignored. - **Serials:** a produced serial is available if it is not in the consumed list. Order follows bundle and row index. - **Batches:** the available quantity is produced minus consumed, and a batch is kept only if that is above 0. 4. **Capping to the row quantity.** `_cap_pool_to_qty` takes the first `int(qty)` serials if the pool has serials. Otherwise it walks the batches in order, taking `min(batch_qty, remaining)` from each until the quantity is met. 5. **Creating the bundle.** If anything was selected, a `SerialBatchCreation` call creates an Outward Serial and Batch Bundle with `do_not_submit=True`. The row gets `serial_and_batch_bundle = bundle.name` and `use_serial_batch_fields = 0`. **If fewer units are available than the row quantity.** It does not throw. The helper's docstring says it fills what is available and leaves the shortfall blank for the user. The code does this in three ways: - **Partial fill:** the bundle holds only the available serials or batches, which is fewer than the row quantity. - **Row quantity unchanged:** the row keeps its original `qty`. The bundle's `qty` is set to the full `flt(qty)`, so the mismatch is left for the user to correct, and validation will flag it on save or submit. - **Nothing available:** if no serials or batches are available, the function returns without creating a bundle. The row stays empty and the user has to select them manually.

final answer
Still tracing the pre-fill path. I've now read all of its helper functions, so I can answer. **Entry point.** `make_stock_entry` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/job_card/mapper.py` maps the Job Card to a "Material Transfer for Manufacture" Stock Entry. In its `set_missing_values` step it calls `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(target, item)` for each item row. `ManufactureEntry.add_raw_materials` calls the same function. **Function:** `set_previous_operation_serial_batch` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/manufacturing.py`. **When it does nothing.** It returns without changes in these cases: - The Stock Entry has no `work_order`. - The row has no `s_warehouse`. - The row's qty is 0 or less. - The row already has a `serial_and_batch_bundle`, `serial_no` or `batch_no`. **How it picks the pool.** Otherwise it calls `get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch(work_order, item_code, s_warehouse)`. - **Which items qualify:** the item must be the `finished_good` of some Work Order Operation on that Work Order. It must also have `has_serial_no` or `has_batch_no`. Ordinary raw materials get an empty pool. - **What counts as produced:** `_get_operation_sn_batch(..., produced=True)` reads submitted "Manufacture" Stock Entries for the Work Order. It takes rows with `is_finished_item=1` and `t_warehouse` equal to the source warehouse. `_get_operation_bundles` collects their bundles. The serial and batch entries come from the Serial and Batch Entry table, excluding cancelled ones. - **What counts as consumed:** the same query with `produced=False` looks at submitted Stock Entries of any purpose for that Work Order, where the item left the same warehouse as `s_warehouse`. - **Serial numbers:** those produced and not yet consumed. They keep the order of the bundle entries. - **Batches:** produced quantity minus consumed quantity per batch, kept only where the result is above 0. **Capping to the row quantity.** `_cap_pool_to_qty` takes the row qty multiplied by `conversion_factor`, so it is in stock UOM. - **Serials:** it takes the first `int(qty)` serial numbers. - **Batches:** it walks the batches in order and takes `min(batch_qty, remaining)` from each until the quantity is filled. **What gets set.** It builds an Outward draft bundle with `SerialBatchCreation(...).make_serial_and_batch_bundle()` and `do_not_submit=True`. It then sets `row.serial_and_batch_bundle` to that bundle and sets `row.use_serial_batch_fields = 0`. **If fewer units are available than the row qty.** - **Partial fill:** the bundle holds only what is available. This is the number of serials found, or the batch quantity found. - **Shortfall:** the shortfall is left unfilled for the user to complete. The docstring says "filling what is available and leaving any shortfall blank for the user." - **No error:** the row qty is not reduced and nothing is thrown at this point. - **Nothing available:** if the pool is empty, it returns without creating a bundle, and the row stays blank. One thing I didn't check: the bundle's `qty` is passed as the full row qty, even though only the available serials or batches are listed. I didn't trace how `SerialBatchCreation` handles that mismatch, so I can't say whether it trims the qty or flags it later.

final answer
When a Stock Entry is mapped from a Job Card, each item row goes through `set_previous_operation_serial_batch`. It pre-fills the serial numbers or batches that an earlier operation produced and that are still in the row's source warehouse. If fewer units exist than the row needs, it fills what is there and leaves the rest blank. I read the code but did not run it. **Where it is called** - `make_stock_entry` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/job_card/mapper.py` builds the "Material Transfer for Manufacture" Stock Entry. Its `set_missing_values` step calls `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(target, item)` for every row. - `stock_entry_type.py` also calls the same function. **The function** (`erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/manufacturing.py`, `set_previous_operation_serial_batch`) - It does nothing in these cases: - the Stock Entry has no `work_order`; - the row has no `s_warehouse`; - the row quantity is 0 or less; - the row already has a `serial_and_batch_bundle`, `serial_no` or `batch_no`. - The quantity it works with is `qty × conversion_factor`, which is the stock-UOM quantity. - It then calls `get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch(work_order, item_code, warehouse)` to get the pool of candidates. **How the pool is chosen** (`get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch`) - Only semi-finished goods qualify. The item must be the `finished_good` of some Work Order Operation on that Work Order. Ordinary raw materials get an empty result. - The item must also have `has_serial_no` or `has_batch_no`. - `_get_operation_sn_batch` reads the Serial and Batch Entry rows from two sets of submitted Stock Entry bundles for the same Work Order: - Produced: "Manufacture" entries where the item is a finished item (`is_finished_item = 1`) and the target warehouse is `t_warehouse`. - Consumed: entries where the item left the same warehouse as `s_warehouse`. - Serial numbers in the pool are those produced and not yet consumed. - Batches in the pool are `produced qty − consumed qty`, kept only if that is above 0. **When there are fewer units than the row needs** (`_cap_pool_to_qty`) - For serial numbers, it takes the first `cint(qty)` serials from the pool. If the pool is smaller, it takes all of them. - For batches, it goes through the pool in order and takes `min(batch_qty, remaining)` from each batch until the quantity is met or the batches run out. - If nothing is found, the function returns and the row stays untouched. - Otherwise it creates a draft Outward bundle with `SerialBatchCreation(...).make_serial_and_batch_bundle()` and `do_not_submit=True`. The bundle is created with the full row quantity, even when fewer serials or batches were found. It sets `row.serial_and_batch_bundle` to the new bundle and `use_serial_batch_fields = 0`. - It does not raise an error. The shortfall is left for the user to fill in manually.

final answer
When a Stock Entry is made from a Job Card, ERPNext fills the serial numbers or batches of a semi-finished-good input from what earlier operations of the same Work Order produced and left in the source warehouse. If fewer units are available than the row's quantity, it fills what exists and leaves the rest blank. I read the code but did not run it. **Where it starts** - `make_stock_entry` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/job_card/mapper.py` maps the Job Card to a Stock Entry with purpose "Material Transfer for Manufacture". - Its `set_missing_values` step loops over the Stock Entry items and calls `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(target, item)` for each one. **How it decides: `set_previous_operation_serial_batch` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/manufacturing.py`** - The row's quantity is `qty × conversion_factor`. - The function does nothing in these cases: - there is no work order; - the row has no source warehouse; - the quantity is zero or less; - the row already has a serial and batch bundle, serial number or batch number. - Otherwise it calls `get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch(work_order, item_code, s_warehouse)`. **What counts as available: `get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch`** - The item must be the `finished_good` of some Work Order Operation in that Work Order. Ordinary raw materials return an empty result. - The item must also have serial numbers or batches enabled. - It then compares two sets, both taken from submitted Stock Entries of the same Work Order through `_get_operation_bundles` and `_get_operation_sn_batch`: - **Produced:** entries with purpose "Manufacture", marked as finished items, whose target warehouse is the source warehouse. - **Consumed:** entries that took the item out of that warehouse as their source warehouse. - For serial numbers, the pool is the produced serials that are not in the consumed set. - For batches, the pool is the produced quantity minus the consumed quantity per batch, keeping only batches with a positive balance. **If fewer units are available than the row's quantity** - `_cap_pool_to_qty(pool, qty)` trims the pool to the row's quantity. Serials are cut with `serial_nos[:int(qty)]`. - Batches are taken one after another, each up to `min(batch_qty, remaining)`, until the quantity is reached or the pool runs out. If the pool is smaller than the quantity, you get a partial list. - If nothing is available, the function returns and the row is left untouched. - Otherwise it builds an Outward "Serial and Batch Bundle" with `SerialBatchCreation(... do_not_submit=True).make_serial_and_batch_bundle()`. - The bundle's `qty` is the full row quantity. Only the serials or batches actually found are filled in. - The bundle is set on the row's `serial_and_batch_bundle`, with `use_serial_batch_fields = 0`. - No error is raised for the shortfall. The docstring says it leaves "any shortfall blank for the user". The user has to complete or correct the bundle before submitting.
Q24Group E — Concurrency & database semanticsSome ERPNext flows perform a best-effort or optional database write inside a larger transaction — for example auto-creating a link…
Some ERPNext flows perform a best-effort or optional database write inside a larger transaction — for example auto-creating a linked document while submitting another. On PostgreSQL, if such a write fails, every later statement in the transaction fails too. What mechanism does the codebase use to survive these failures, how does it differ from a full rollback, and where is it applied? Name concrete functions and files.

final answer
The codebase uses **database savepoints**. It calls `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` before the risky write. If the write fails, it calls `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. I read the code in `stock/utils.py` and the subcontracting mapper. The rest of the list below comes from a grep for `savepoint` and the comments it showed, so I haven't opened those files. **How it differs from a full rollback** - A plain `frappe.db.rollback()` ends the whole transaction. It discards everything done earlier in the request, such as the parent document, the Customer, or earlier rows in a loop. It also drops any pending `on_commit` and `on_rollback` hooks. - On PostgreSQL, a failed statement puts the transaction into an aborted state. Every later statement then fails with "current transaction is aborted" until you roll back. MariaDB doesn't behave this way. - A savepoint rolls back only to the marker. Work done before it is kept, and the transaction becomes usable again. The comments in the code say this directly. Examples are "preserve transaction in postgres" in `stock/utils.py` and "a failure here does NOT discard the Customer" in `crm/frappe_crm_api.py`. **Where it is applied** - **`erpnext/stock/utils.py`, `_create_bin`:** it sets the `create_bin` savepoint, inserts the Bin, and on `frappe.UniqueValidationError` rolls back to the savepoint. It then fetches the Bin that a concurrent request created. - **Best-effort submit while mapping documents:** - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py` uses the `submit_subcontracting_receipt` savepoint. If `target_doc.submit()` fails, it rolls back to the savepoint and adds a "Submit Action Failed" comment to the document. - `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py` uses `submit_subcontracting_order`. - **Optional linked or auto-created documents:** - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py` uses `auto_create_fiscal_year` for a possible duplicate year. - `erpnext/crm/frappe_crm_api.py` uses `crm_create_address` and `crm_customer_links`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py` uses `subscription_update`. - `erpnext/stock/reorder_item.py` uses `reorder_mr`. - `erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/install_fixtures.py` uses `create_bank_account`. - `erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/taxes_setup.py` uses `regional_tax_settings`. - `erpnext/telephony/doctype/call_log/call_log.py` uses `link_call_logs`. - `erpnext/crm/doctype/email_campaign/email_campaign.py` uses `email_campaign_send`. - **Per-item isolation in loops, so one failure doesn't undo the others:** - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/opening_invoice_creation_tool/opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` uses a savepoint named with `frappe.generate_hash`. - `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py` uses `plaid_bank_account`, `plaid_update_account` and `plaid_sync_txn`. - `erpnext/utilities/bulk_transaction.py` uses `before_creation_state`. - `erpnext/accounts/bulk_payment.py` uses `bulk_pe`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/ledger_merge/ledger_merge.py` uses `ledger_merge_row`. - `erpnext/regional/doctype/import_supplier_invoice/import_supplier_invoice.py` uses `import_invoice`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_transaction/bank_transaction_upload.py` uses `bank_entry`. - `erpnext/assets/doctype/asset/depreciation.py` uses `depr_entry`. - `erpnext/stock/doctype/repost_item_valuation/repost_item_valuation.py` uses `repost_accounting_ledger`. - `erpnext/stock/report/stock_and_account_value_comparison/stock_and_account_value_comparison.py` uses `repost_value_comparison` and `repost_based_on_transaction`. - **Previews that must never persist anything:** `erpnext/controllers/ledger_preview.py` uses the `ledger_preview` savepoint. - **Patches:** `erpnext/patches/v13_0/trim_whitespace_from_serial_nos.py` uses the `serial_no_patch` savepoint. The repo's `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py` also mentions "savepoint discipline" as something it checks for.

final answer
**Mechanism:** the codebase uses database savepoints through Frappe's `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` and `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. I identified this from the call sites and their comments. I didn't open Frappe's implementation, so the SQL-level details below are from general PostgreSQL behaviour. **The pattern:** 1. Call `frappe.db.savepoint("<name>")` just before the optional write. 2. Run the write inside `try`. 3. On failure, call `frappe.db.rollback(save_point="<name>")`. Then either log the error, fall back to an alternative, or skip that item. **Why it's needed:** on PostgreSQL, a failed statement such as a duplicate-key INSERT puts the whole transaction into an aborted state. Every later statement then fails until a rollback. Rolling back to a savepoint clears that state for the failed write only, so the outer transaction can continue. **How it differs from a full rollback:** - A plain `frappe.db.rollback()` discards everything since the last commit. That includes the parent document, earlier loop iterations and queued side effects. - A savepoint rollback undoes only the work done after the savepoint was set. Everything earlier in the transaction survives. - The `crm_customer_links` comment in `erpnext/crm/frappe_crm_api.py` says the same thing: a failure while linking contacts and addresses must not discard the Customer just created. `opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` likewise scopes each invoice to its own savepoint, so a failure undoes only that invoice. **Where it's applied** (all in `erpnext/`): | File | Function or location | Savepoint | What it does | |---|---|---|---| | `stock/utils.py` | `_create_bin` | `create_bin` | On `UniqueValidationError` from a concurrent Bin insert, rolls back to the savepoint (commented "preserve transaction in postgres"), then fetches the existing Bin with `get_last_doc`. | | `stock/report/stock_and_account_value_comparison/stock_and_account_value_comparison.py` | `repost_based_on_transaction` | `repost_based_on_transaction` | Wraps each Repost Item Valuation submit and, on `DuplicateEntryError`, rolls back to the savepoint. The comment says a failed insert would otherwise kill the rest of the loop. The same file also uses `repost_value_comparison`. | | `utilities/bulk_transaction.py` | `job`, `retry_failed_transactions` | `before_creation_state` | Isolates each document in a bulk conversion. On failure it rolls back to the savepoint and writes a failure log. | | `accounts/doctype/opening_invoice_creation_tool/opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` | per-invoice creation | `opening_invoice_<hash>` | One savepoint per invoice, so a failure undoes only that invoice. | | `accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py` | auto-creation of a Fiscal Year | `auto_create_fiscal_year` | Guards the auto-created Fiscal Year against a duplicate-year insert. | | `buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py` | auto-submit of a Subcontracting Order | `submit_subcontracting_order` | Protects the Purchase Order submit from a failure in the linked document. | | `subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py` | auto-submit of a linked document | `submit_subcontracting_receipt` | Same purpose as the Purchase Order mapper. | | `accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py` | subscription update | `subscription_update` | Isolates the subscription update. | | `crm/frappe_crm_api.py` | address and link creation | `crm_create_address`, `crm_customer_links` | Keeps the Customer when address or contact linking fails. | | `telephony/doctype/call_log/call_log.py` | call-log linking | `link_call_logs` | Isolates the optional linking write. | | `crm/doctype/email_campaign/email_campaign.py` | campaign send | `email_campaign_send` | Isolates the send. | | `erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py` | bank sync | `plaid_bank_account`, `plaid_update_account`, `plaid_sync_txn` | Per-account and per-transaction savepoints, so a failed insert or submit doesn't discard the Bank. | | `setup/setup_wizard/operations/taxes_setup.py` | `update_regional_tax_settings` | `regional_tax_settings` | Rolls back to the savepoint and calls `frappe.log_error` instead of failing setup. | | `setup/setup_wizard/operations/install_fixtures.py` | bank account creation | `create_bank_account` | Isolates the optional bank account insert. | | `setup/demo.py` | `setup_demo_data` | `demo_data` | Rolls back to the savepoint, logs the error and sends a notification. | | `accounts/bulk_payment.py` | bulk payment entries | `bulk_pe` | Per-entry isolation. | | `accounts/doctype/bank_transaction/bank_transaction_upload.py` | bank entries | `bank_entry` | Per-entry final answer
ERPNext handles this with **database savepoints**. It calls `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` before the risky write. If the write fails, it calls `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. I identified the call sites with a grep for those calls. I did not open each function, so the specific behaviors below come from the savepoint names and file locations, plus a few comments in the code. **Why it's needed.** On PostgreSQL, a failed statement puts the whole transaction into an aborted state, and every later statement errors. MariaDB doesn't do this. A plain `try/except` around the write is therefore not enough on Postgres. **How it differs from a full rollback.** Calling `frappe.db.rollback()` with no arguments undoes the entire transaction. That would discard the parent document's work, such as the submit that was in progress. `rollback(save_point=...)` only issues `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT`. It undoes the writes made since the savepoint and clears the aborted state. The outer transaction stays open and its earlier writes are kept. Several comments in the code say `# preserve transaction in postgres`. The usual pattern is: ```python frappe.db.savepoint("x") try: <optional write> except Exception: frappe.db.rollback(save_point="x") ``` The `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py` checker mentions "savepoint discipline" as a Postgres issue that needs review. **Where it is applied.** The first group below are the best-effort or optional writes the question describes: - **Subcontracting auto-submit:** - `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py`, savepoint `submit_subcontracting_order`. - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py`, savepoint `submit_subcontracting_receipt`. - **Payment and subscription updates:** - `accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py`, savepoint `subscription_update`. - `accounts/bulk_payment.py`, savepoint `bulk_pe`. - **Stock helpers:** - `stock/utils.py`, savepoint `bin_creation_savepoint`, for creating a Bin. - `stock/reorder_item.py`, savepoint `reorder_mr`, for auto-creating Material Requests. - `stock/doctype/repost_item_valuation/repost_item_valuation.py`, savepoint `repost_accounting_ledger`. - **Other document creation:** - `accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py`, savepoint `auto_create_fiscal_year`. - `assets/doctype/asset/depreciation.py`, savepoint `depr_entry`. - `accounts/doctype/bank_transaction/bank_transaction_upload.py`, savepoint `bank_entry`. - `accounts/doctype/ledger_merge/ledger_merge.py`, savepoint `ledger_merge_row`. - `accounts/doctype/opening_invoice_creation_tool/opening_invoice_creation_tool.py`, a savepoint whose name is passed in. - `regional/doctype/import_supplier_invoice/import_supplier_invoice.py`, savepoint `import_invoice`. - `telephony/doctype/call_log/call_log.py`, savepoint `link_call_logs`. - `crm/doctype/email_campaign/email_campaign.py`, savepoint `email_campaign_send`. - `crm/frappe_crm_api.py`, savepoints `crm_create_address` and `crm_customer_links`. - **Setup and integrations:** - `setup/setup_wizard/operations/install_fixtures.py`, savepoint `create_bank_account`. - `setup/setup_wizard/operations/taxes_setup.py`, savepoint `regional_tax_settings`. - `erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py`, savepoints `plaid_bank_account`, `plaid_update_account` and `plaid_sync_txn`. - **Bulk operations:** `utilities/bulk_transaction.py`, savepoint `before_creation_state`. - **Other uses:** - `controllers/ledger_preview.py` uses savepoint `ledger_preview` for GL and stock ledger previews. The savepoint is rolled back deliberately, so the preview never persists. - `setup/demo.py` uses savepoint `demo_data`. - The stock ledger invariant check and the stock and account value comparison reports use savepoints for their repost operations. - A patch uses one: `patches/v13_0/trim_whitespace_from_serial_nos.py`. - Tests use them to survive expected duplicate-entry errors: `test_routing.py`, `test_item.py` (`dup_barcode`), `test_bin.py` (`dup_bin`) and `test_supplier_quotation.py`.

final answer
The mechanism is a database savepoint. The code calls `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` before the risky write. If the write fails, it calls `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. I read two of the call sites (`erpnext/stock/utils.py` and `fiscal_year.py`) in full. For the rest I only saw the grep hits and comments, not the surrounding code. **How it differs from a full rollback** - A plain `frappe.db.rollback()` ends the whole transaction. That discards everything done earlier in the request, such as the document being submitted, and anything already queued. - `rollback(save_point=...)` only undoes work back to that marker. The transaction stays open and earlier work is kept. - On PostgreSQL, a failed statement puts the transaction into an aborted state, so every later statement fails until a rollback. Rolling back to the savepoint clears that state. MariaDB doesn't abort the transaction this way, so this matters mainly on Postgres. **Where it is applied** - **`_create_bin`** in `erpnext/stock/utils.py`: it sets a savepoint named `create_bin` and inserts the Bin. On `frappe.UniqueValidationError` (a concurrent insert), it rolls back to the savepoint, with the comment "preserve transaction in postgres". It then fetches the existing Bin with `get_last_doc`. - **Fiscal Year auto-creation** in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py`: it uses the savepoint `auto_create_fiscal_year`. A duplicate-year insert would otherwise poison the whole scheduler transaction and kill the next loop iteration. - **Plaid sync** in `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py`: it uses the savepoints `plaid_bank_account`, `plaid_update_account` and `plaid_sync_txn`. A failed per-transaction insert or submit must not discard the Bank Account. - **CRM API** in `erpnext/crm/frappe_crm_api.py`: `crm_create_address` and `crm_customer_links`. A failure while linking contacts or addresses must not discard the Customer. - **Submit-time auto-creation:** - `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py` uses `submit_subcontracting_order`. - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py` uses `submit_subcontracting_receipt`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py` uses `subscription_update`. - **Bulk or per-row loops:** - `erpnext/utilities/bulk_transaction.py` uses `before_creation_state`. - `erpnext/accounts/bulk_payment.py` uses `bulk_pe`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/opening_invoice_creation_tool/opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` uses a hash-suffixed `opening_invoice_<hash>` savepoint. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/ledger_merge/ledger_merge.py` uses `ledger_merge_row`. - `erpnext/stock/doctype/repost_item_valuation/repost_item_valuation.py` uses `repost_accounting_ledger`. - `erpnext/stock/report/stock_and_account_value_comparison/stock_and_account_value_comparison.py` uses `repost_based_on_transaction`. Its comment says rolling back to the savepoint keeps the other reposts intact. - `erpnext/stock/reorder_item.py` uses `reorder_mr`. - **Other flows:** - `erpnext/crm/doctype/email_campaign/email_campaign.py` uses `email_campaign_send`. - `erpnext/telephony/doctype/call_log/call_log.py` uses `link_call_logs`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_transaction/bank_transaction_upload.py` uses `bank_entry`. - `erpnext/regional/doctype/import_supplier_invoice/import_supplier_invoice.py` uses `import_invoice`. - `erpnext/assets/doctype/asset/depreciation.py` uses `depr_entry`. - `erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/install_fixtures.py` uses `create_bank_account`. - `erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/taxes_setup.py` uses `regional_tax_settings`. - `erpnext/setup/demo.py` uses `demo_data`. - **A different use, `erpnext/controllers/ledger_preview.py`:** it uses the `ledger_preview` savepoint so the preview never persists anything. - **A patch:** `erpnext/patches/v13_0/trim_whitespace_from_serial_nos.py` uses `serial_no_patch`. - **Guard script:** `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py` mentions "savepoint discipline" as something it can't check automatically.

final answer
ERPNext doesn't use `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST` anywhere. I searched the `.py` files for `nulls_first`, `nulls_last` and `NULLS FIRST/LAST` and found no uses. Instead it forces the same NULL position on both databases with three techniques in the query builder (`frappe.qb`, which is PyPika). **1. Add a boolean `IS NULL` sort key before the real column** `col.isnull()` evaluates to 0 or 1 on both engines, so adding it as the leading `ORDER BY` key fixes where NULLs land. - **Latest-price lookups** in `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py`. Two queries order by `ip.valid_from.isnull()` ascending, so rows with a date come first, then `ip.valid_from` descending. These pick the "latest valid" Item Price, so a NULL `valid_from` must not win. - The first is in the price-lookup function around line 1132, which adds `creation desc` as a tiebreaker. - The second is around line 1233, with `IfNull(batch_no, "")` and `uom` as tiebreakers and `.limit(1)`. - **POS item prices** in `erpnext/selling/page/point_of_sale/point_of_sale.py` (about line 234). It uses `ItemPrice.valid_from.isnull()` ascending, then `valid_from` descending. - **Serial number picking** in `get_serial_nos_based_on_filters` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/serial_and_batch_bundle/serial_and_batch_bundle.py` (about line 2511). For FIFO ordering by `amc_expiry_date`, it orders by `isnull()` descending, then the column ascending. NULL (no AMC expiry) is placed first, matching MariaDB's ascending behaviour, and this decides which serial numbers are picked under the `limit`. **2. Replace NULL with a sentinel using `IfNull` or `COALESCE`** - **Email Digest to-do list**, `EmailDigest.get_todo_list` in `erpnext/setup/doctype/email_digest/email_digest.py`. - It orders by `IfNull(todo.date, "1000-01-01")`, with the comment "NULL dates first, as MariaDB `date asc` did". - The priority sort uses a `Case()` expression that reproduces MySQL's `FIELD(priority, 'High', 'Medium', 'Low')`. Unknown, empty or NULL priority maps to 0 and sorts first. - The test `test_email_digest.py` asserts both behaviours. - **Certification ordering** in `erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py` (line 24). It orders by `IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01")` descending. - **Warehouse and batch ordering** in `erpnext/controllers/queries.py` (line 914). It orders by `IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0)` descending. In `get_item_details.py` the Item Price query orders by `IfNull(ip.batch_no, "")` descending. **3. Filter NULLs out so the ordering no longer matters** - **First contact date** in `erpnext/crm/report/lead_conversion_time/lead_conversion_time.py` (about lines 121–123). The query adds `comm.communication_date.isnotnull()` before `orderby(comm.communication_date)`. - Without the filter, MariaDB (NULLs first) returned a NULL-dated Communication as `first_contact`, which gave a wrong duration. Postgres (NULLs last) returned the earliest real date. - `test_lead_conversion_time.py` documents this. - **Expiry and manufacturing dates** in `erpnext/controllers/queries.py`. The batch queries use `expiry_date >= x OR expiry_date.isnull()` filters rather than relying on sort order.

final answer
ERPNext doesn't use `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST` anywhere in its Python code. A grep for those keywords found nothing. It controls NULL ordering in the query itself, with three mechanisms. The sites below came from keyword searches (`orderby` with `IfNull`, `Coalesce` or `isnull`, plus raw `order by` with `coalesce`). I read the surrounding code at the first two, the Serial No query and the Item Price query in `get_item_details.py`. I haven't checked every `orderby` in the repo, so there may be other sites. **1. A leading `col IS NULL` sort key.** `.orderby(col.isnull(), order=...)` adds a boolean first sort key. It is false for real values and true for NULL. Ascending puts NULLs last on both engines, and descending puts them first. The real column is then sorted as the next key. - `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py`, lines 1132–1134: `orderby(ip.valid_from.isnull(), asc)`, then `valid_from desc`, then `creation desc`. The query picks the matching Item Price, so a price with no `valid_from` has to rank last on both engines. - `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py`, line 1233: the same `valid_from.isnull()` pattern in a second Item Price lookup. - `erpnext/selling/page/point_of_sale/point_of_sale.py`, line 234: `orderby(ItemPrice.valid_from.isnull(), asc)`. - `erpnext/stock/doctype/serial_and_batch_bundle/serial_and_batch_bundle.py`, lines 2510–2512, in `get_serial_nos_based_on_filters`. For FIFO picking by `amc_expiry_date`, it sorts `isnull()` descending, so NULLs come first, and then the column ascending. This reproduces MariaDB's NULLs-first behaviour. The query uses `LIMIT qty ... FOR UPDATE`, so the ordering decides which serial numbers get picked. **2. A sentinel value through `IfNull` or `Coalesce`.** The NULL is replaced with a value that sorts where MariaDB would put it. - `erpnext/setup/doctype/email_digest/email_digest.py`, `get_todo_list`, lines 226–240. It uses `orderby(IfNull(todo.date, "1000-01-01"))` so NULL dates sort first, as on MariaDB. It also uses a `Case` expression with `else_(0)` to imitate MySQL's `FIELD(priority, 'High', 'Medium', 'Low')`, where unknown, empty or NULL priority returns 0 and sorts first. The query has `LIMIT 20`, so this decides which rows are returned. `test_email_digest.py::test_get_todo_list_priority_and_date_ordering` checks both cases. - `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py`, lines 1235, 1250 and 1255: `IfNull(ip.batch_no/customer/supplier, "")` descending, so NULLs sort as the empty string. - `erpnext/controllers/queries.py`, line 914: `IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0)` descending. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py`, line 2798: `Coalesce(PR.transaction_date, PR.creation)` ascending. - `erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py`, line 24: `IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01")` descending. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/utils.py`, line 155: the raw SQL `order by coalesce(priority, '') desc, ...`. **3. Filtering the NULLs out.** When the first row is taken from the ordered result, the query excludes NULLs so both engines agree. - `erpnext/crm/report/lead_conversion_time/lead_conversion_time.py`, lines 119–125. The `first_contact` query has `.where(... & comm.communication_date.isnotnull()).orderby(comm.communication_date).limit(1)`. Without the filter, MariaDB (NULLs first) returned a NULL-dated Communication and a wrong duration, while PostgreSQL (NULLs last) returned the earliest real date. `test_lead_conversion_time.py::test_first_contact_ignores_null_communication_date` covers this.

final answer
ERPNext never uses `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST`. A grep for those keywords across the Python files found nothing. Instead it controls NULL ordering in three ways, depending on the query. I read the code at the payment entry, pricing rule, lead conversion and email digest sites. I only saw the grep lines for `get_item_details.py`, `payment_setup_certification.py` and `controllers/queries.py`, and I did not open those files. **1. Wrap the sort key in `IfNull` or `Coalesce`, so NULL gets a fixed sentinel value.** Both engines then sort the same way, because no NULL reaches the `ORDER BY`. - **`erpnext/setup/doctype/email_digest/email_digest.py` (~L239):** `.orderby(IfNull(todo.date, "1000-01-01"))`. The inline comment says "NULL dates first, as MariaDB `date asc` did", so it reproduces MariaDB's ordering on PostgreSQL. The test `test_get_todo_list_priority_and_date_ordering` in `test_email_digest.py` covers it. - **`erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py` (~L1235, 1250, 1255):** In the price-list rate lookup, `IfNull(ip.batch_no, "")`, `IfNull(ip.customer, "")` and `IfNull(ip.supplier, "")` are sorted `desc`. These decide which Item Price is picked, so rows with a value rank ahead of rows with NULL on both engines. - **`erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py` (L24):** `IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01")` sorted `desc`. - **`erpnext/controllers/queries.py` (L914):** `IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0)` sorted `desc`. - **`erpnext/accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/utils.py` (~L155):** The raw SQL uses `order by coalesce(`tabPricing Rule`.priority, '') desc, `tabPricing Rule`.name desc`. This decides which pricing rule wins, so a NULL priority must sort last when descending. **2. Coalesce to a fallback column, plus unique tiebreakers.** - **`erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py` (~L2798):** The Payment Request allocation query orders by `Coalesce(PR.transaction_date, PR.creation)`, then `PR.creation`, then `PR.name`. A code comment says the tiebreaker is there so Payment Requests sharing a transaction date "allocate in the same order on both engines". This one decides which request gets allocated first. **3. Filter NULLs out in the `WHERE` clause, so they can't be picked.** - **`erpnext/crm/report/lead_conversion_time/lead_conversion_time.py`, `get_communication_details`:** The "first contact" query is `.where(... & comm.communication_date.isnotnull()).orderby(comm.communication_date).limit(1)`. Without the filter, MariaDB would return a NULL row first and PostgreSQL would return the earliest real date. The test `test_first_contact_ignores_null_communication_date` covers it. **Related but not about NULLs.** `Case().when(...).else_(...)` ordering appears in `erpnext/regional/report/irs_1099/irs_1099.py` and `erpnext/projects/utils.py`. It ranks rows by a computed 0/1 key. It is not aimed at NULLs, but it gives the same kind of engine-independent control.

final answer
I've found the query sites and have enough to answer, so I'm writing it up now. ERPNext has no `NULLS FIRST` / `NULLS LAST` anywhere in its Python code. A grep for those found nothing. It controls NULL ordering by changing the ORDER BY expression so the result is the same on both databases. Two techniques do this. ## Mechanism 1: an `IS NULL` flag as a leading sort key The code adds `ORDER BY col IS NULL ASC|DESC` ahead of the real sort key. That flag is 0 or 1 on both engines, so it forces NULLs to the front or back whatever the default is. MariaDB sorts NULLs first on ASC, and PostgreSQL sorts them last. - **Item price lookup, `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py`, line 1132** (the price-selection query for an item): - The sort is `.orderby(ip.valid_from.isnull(), asc).orderby(ip.valid_from, desc).orderby(ip.creation, desc)`. - Prices that have a `valid_from` come first, newest first, and open-ended prices (NULL `valid_from`) come last. - The code then takes the first row that passes the validity checks. - **Item price lookup with `.limit(1)`, `get_item_price`, same file, line 1233:** - It uses the same `valid_from.isnull()` ASC, then `valid_from` DESC. - It adds tiebreakers `IfNull(batch_no, "")` DESC and `uom` DESC, plus `IfNull(customer, "")` or `IfNull(supplier, "")` DESC at lines 1250 and 1255. - Because of `LIMIT 1`, the NULL placement decides which price is returned. - **POS item prices, `erpnext/selling/page/point_of_sale/point_of_sale.py`, line 234:** `.orderby(ItemPrice.valid_from.isnull(), order=Order.asc)`. - **Serial number picking, `get_serial_nos_based_on_filters` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/serial_and_batch_bundle/serial_and_batch_bundle.py`, line 2511:** - For FIFO picking ordered by `amc_expiry_date`, it adds `.orderby(col.isnull(), order=DESC)` before `.orderby(col)`. - NULL (no expiry) serials are picked first, matching MariaDB's ASC behaviour. - The query has `LIMIT qty` and `FOR UPDATE`, so this decides which serial numbers are chosen. - For LIFO it just sorts DESC. ## Mechanism 2: `IfNull` / `Coalesce` with a sentinel value The code replaces NULL with a fixed value in the ORDER BY, so NULL sorts at a known position. - **`erpnext/setup/doctype/email_digest/email_digest.py`, line 239:** `.orderby(IfNull(todo.date, "1000-01-01"))`. The comment says "NULL dates first, as MariaDB `date asc` did". This is the clearest case of copying MariaDB's behaviour. - **`erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py`, line 24:** `IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01")` DESC, which puts NULL last. - **`erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py`, line 2798:** `Coalesce(PR.transaction_date, PR.creation)` ASC. - **`erpnext/controllers/queries.py`, line 914 (the warehouse link query):** `IfNull(bin.actual_qty, 0)` DESC, then warehouse name ASC. This applies to the left-joined `bin_dt` (Bin) table, where there may be no matching row. - **Pricing rule selection, `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/utils.py`, line 155:** raw SQL `order by coalesce(priority, '') desc, name desc`. The pricing rule picked depends on where NULL priorities sort. - **Item price tiebreakers:** the `IfNull(batch_no, "")`, `IfNull(customer, "")` and `IfNull(supplier, "")` sorts in `get_item_details.py` are also sentinel-style. ## Guard rail `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py` is a pre-commit check for MySQL-only SQL. Its docstring says it does not catch NULL ordering or `ORDER BY ... LIMIT 1` tiebreakers. Those need a Postgres test run or a human reviewer, which is why the sites above carry explicit fixes. I found these sites with grep for `isnull()` / `IfNull` / `Coalesce` inside `orderby`. Other sites that don't use those patterns could exist.

final answer
**No.** ERPNext does not block an expired batch on a Stock Entry that issues or transfers stock. The expiry check exists, but it explicitly skips Material Issue and Material Transfer. **The check that exists:** `validate_serialized_batch` in `erpnext/stock/services/serial_batch_bundle_service.py` (around lines 106–147). - For a row with a positive `qty` and a `batch_no`, it reads the Batch's `expiry_date`. - If `getdate(expiry_date) < getdate(doc.posting_date)`, it throws `"Row #{0}: The batch {1} has already expired."` with `BatchExpiredError`. - It only runs while `docstatus < 2`. **Why Stock Entry issues and transfers escape it:** just before that check, the function sets `is_material_issue = True` when the document is a Stock Entry with purpose "Material Issue" or "Material Transfer". It then runs `continue` for those rows, so the expiry validation is never reached. The expiry throw therefore only applies to other documents and to Stock Entry purposes such as Material Receipt, Manufacture, or Repack. I read this function but did not trace which doctypes call it. **The second check also skips Stock Entry:** `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_ledger_entry/stock_ledger_entry.py` (around lines 287–299). It throws `"Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired."` when `posting_date` is after `expiry_date`. It begins with `if self.batch_no and self.voucher_type != "Stock Entry"`, so Stock Entry ledger entries are excluded. It also exempts purchase returns and sales returns. **What exists instead is only a picker-side filter, not a validation:** `get_non_expired_batches` and the `expiry_date >= today() OR expiry_date IS NULL` filters in `erpnext/stock/doctype/serial_and_batch_bundle/serial_and_batch_bundle.py` (around lines 2570–2580, 3173 and 3518). They hide expired batches when batches are auto-picked or listed, for example for FIFO or expiry-based picking. A user can still enter an expired batch manually, and nothing throws when the Stock Entry is submitted.

final answer
**Mostly no.** ERPNext has no general check that throws on an expired batch for a Stock Entry. A check exists only for some purposes, and it runs in `validate`, not at submit. **What exists** - `StockEntry.validate()` calls `StockEntry.validate_batch()` (`stock_entry.py`, around lines 310 and 1479). - That method loops over the item rows only when `purpose` is one of "Material Transfer for Manufacture", "Manufacture", "Repack" or "Send to Subcontractor". - For each row it calls `StockEntryDetail.validate_batch()` (`stock_entry_detail/stock_entry_detail.py`, lines 88-99). - That row method throws "Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired." if `getdate(parent.posting_date) > getdate(batch.expiry_date)`. It also throws if the batch is disabled. - Because `validate` also runs when the document is submitted, this check does fire at submit for those purposes. **What is not covered** - Plain "Material Issue" and "Material Transfer" entries are not in that purpose list, so this check does not run for them. - `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch()` (`stock_ledger_entry.py`, lines 287-299) does throw "Batch … has expired" on submit. It starts with `if self.batch_no and self.voucher_type != "Stock Entry"`, so it explicitly skips Stock Entries. It applies to Delivery Note, Sales Invoice and Purchase Receipt vouchers. - Newer Stock Entries use Serial and Batch Bundles, and I found no expiry throw in that path. `get_non_expired_batches` in `serial_and_batch_bundle.py` is only used to filter serial-number availability. **Soft handling for Stock Entry** - `get_expired_batches()` and `get_expired_batch_items()` in `stock_entry/services/serial_batch.py` are called from `stock_entry.js` (around line 520). - As far as I can tell they only support client-side filtering or warning, not a server throw. **Related check in Pick List** - `PickList.validate_expired_batches()` (`pick_list.py`) throws "The following batches are expired, please restock them" for batches with an expiry date on or before today. This is for Pick Lists, not Stock Entries. I did not check the Serial and Batch Bundle validation code for any expiry check beyond searching for "has expired".

final answer
Yes. ERPNext throws an error when a Stock Entry uses an expired batch. Three checks apply, and they cover different purposes. **1. Stock Ledger Entry check (all purposes, including Material Issue and Material Transfer)** - `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch()` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_ledger_entry/stock_ledger_entry.py` (~L287–298). - It runs from the SLE's validate step (L92), which happens when a Stock Entry is submitted. - It reads `Batch.expiry_date` and throws `"Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired."` if `getdate(posting_date) > getdate(expiry_date)`. - I only read the grep output for this one, not the surrounding code. I didn't check whether it skips any entry types, such as cancellations or incoming rows. **2. Serial and Batch Bundle check (the main one in current versions)** - This is in `erpnext/stock/services/serial_batch_bundle_service.py` (~L130–147). - For each row it skips Material Issue (`is_material_issue` → `continue`). - For the other rows it throws `"Row #{idx}: The batch {batch} has already expired."` with `BatchExpiredError`. This happens when `qty > 0`, a `batch_no` is set, `docstatus < 2`, and `expiry_date < posting_date`. - The skip means Material Issue is not blocked by this check. It is still caught by the SLE check above. **3. Stock Entry Detail check (only some purposes)** - `StockEntryDetail.validate_batch()` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry_detail/stock_entry_detail.py` (L88–99). - It first throws if the batch is disabled. It then throws `"Batch … has expired."` if `posting_date > expiry_date`. - `StockEntry.validate_batch()` (`stock_entry.py` ~L1479, called at L310) only calls it for these purposes: - Material Transfer for Manufacture - Manufacture - Repack - Send to Subcontractor - A plain Material Issue or Material Transfer does not go through this one. **Other points** - The comparison uses the Stock Entry's `posting_date`, not today's date. A batch that expires on the posting date is still allowed, because the check is strictly greater-than. - Batches with no `expiry_date` are never blocked. - Batch pickers also filter out expired batches: `serial_and_batch_bundle.py` (L3173, L3518), `stock_entry/services/serial_batch.py` (L363) and `pick_list.py` (L299). That only hides them in the UI. The validations above are what enforce it. - `test_stock_entry.py` has tests that use expired batches (~L1969 and ~L3108).

final answer
**Short answer: no, not for Material Issue or Material Transfer.** ERPNext has expired-batch checks, but the ones that could apply to a Stock Entry skip those two purposes. **1. The Stock Entry validation explicitly skips them.** - `SerialBatchBundleService.validate_serialized_batch()` (`erpnext/stock/services/serial_batch_bundle_service.py`, around lines 106–147) is called from `StockEntry.validate()`. - It does throw `BatchExpiredError` ("Row #{0}: The batch {1} has already expired.") when the batch's `expiry_date` is earlier than the document's `posting_date`. - But it sets `is_material_issue = True` when the doctype is Stock Entry and the purpose is "Material Issue" or "Material Transfer". For those rows it hits `continue` before the expiry check. - So the expiry check only runs for the other Stock Entry purposes, such as Manufacture, Repack, Send to Subcontractor and Material Receipt. - It also only runs for rows that have `qty > 0` and a `batch_no` field. - The check also sits inside the `if hasattr(d, "serial_no") and hasattr(d, "batch_no") and d.serial_no and d.batch_no:` block, so it only covers rows that have both a serial number and a batch. - That `if` block (line 115) encloses the `continue` and the expiry check as I read the code. I didn't re-read the indentation to confirm it. **2. The Stock Ledger Entry check also excludes Stock Entry.** - `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch()` (`erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_ledger_entry/stock_ledger_entry.py:287`) throws "Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired." when `posting_date > expiry_date`. - It is guarded by `self.voucher_type != "Stock Entry"`, so it never fires for Stock Entry vouchers. It does apply to Purchase Receipt, Delivery Note and similar vouchers. **3. `StockEntry.validate_batch()` is unrelated to expiry.** - `StockEntry.validate_batch()` (`stock_entry.py:1479`) only runs for Material Transfer for Manufacture, Manufacture, Repack and Send to Subcontractor. - It delegates to the row's `item.validate_batch()` (the Stock Entry Detail row). I didn't open that method, so I can't say what it checks. **What does stop expired batches being picked:** - **Selection filters:** the Serial and Batch Bundle batch pickers exclude expired batches. `get_non_expired_batches` (`serial_and_batch_bundle.py:2570`) and the `expiry_date >= today() or null` query filters near lines 3173 and 3518 do this. This is a UI and auto-pick filter, not a submit-time throw. A user can still enter an expired batch by hand. - **Pick List:** `PickList.validate_expired_batches()` (`pick_list.py:286`) throws for expired batches on a Pick List. That is a different doctype from Stock Entry. **Conclusion:** there is no submit-time throw for an expired batch on a Stock Entry of purpose Material Issue or Material Transfer. Enforcement would need a custom validation or a hook.

No. ERPNext doesn't email the customer automatically when a Sales Order is submitted, and I found no code in the submit flow that does. **What the submit flow does** - `SalesOrder.on_submit` is in `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py` (around line 450). - It runs `update_prevdoc_status()` and `check_credit_limit()`. - It then runs `update_reserved_qty()` and `DeliveryScheduleService(...).delete_removed_delivery_schedule_items()`. - Next it runs `Authorization Control.validate_approving_authority(...)`, `update_project()`, `update_prevdoc_status("submit")` and `update_blanket_order()`. - None of these steps send an email or notification. - The parent classes (`SellingController`, `StockController`, `AccountsController`, `TransactionBase`, `StatusUpdater`) hold shared submit logic such as status updates. I did not read their `on_submit` code, so I can't rule out email logic there. Their documented roles don't suggest any. - A search of the `sales_order` folder for `sendmail` or `send_email` found nothing in the module code. The only match is a test fixture with `"send_email_alert": 0`. - A search for Sales Order entries under any `notification` folder found none. I did not check for a shipped notification fixture elsewhere in the repo. **How a confirmation email would be sent** - **Manual send:** the user clicks Email on the Sales Order, or uses the print/email dialog. That goes through Frappe's standard `frappe.core.doctype.communication.email.make` flow. - **Configured notification:** an admin can create a Notification record (Frappe's notification doctype) for Sales Order with the event "Submit". The Frappe framework then fires it from its document event hooks after `on_submit`, using a recipient such as `contact_email`. This lives in Frappe core and in configuration, not in ERPNext code. I haven't confirmed how Frappe dispatches it, so treat that mechanism as the usual setup rather than something I verified here. - **Auto Repeat:** recurring Sales Orders can email the new document. That is a separate, opt-in feature.

final answer
**No. Purchase Order has no automatic spend-limit check against the supplier.** I searched `erpnext/buying` for `credit_limit` and `check_credit_limit` and found no matches. `check_credit_limit` is defined only in sales-side and accounting documents: - `selling/doctype/customer/customer.py` (the shared function) - `sales_order.py` - `sales_invoice.py` - `delivery_note.py` - `journal_entry.py` **What gates Purchase Order submission instead** `PurchaseOrder.validate_supplier()` in `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/purchase_order.py` (around line 272) is called from `validate()` (line 200). It reads two flags on the Supplier and checks them against the Supplier Scorecard: - **`prevent_pos`:** If this is set and the supplier has a scorecard standing (`Supplier Scorecard.status`), it calls `frappe.throw`. The Purchase Order cannot be saved or submitted. - **`warn_pos`:** If this is set, it shows only a `frappe.msgprint` caution (orange indicator). The Purchase Order is not blocked. **How it differs from a credit limit** - **What it measures:** A credit limit is a monetary check. It compares the customer's outstanding amount, plus open orders and the new document, against a configured amount per company. The Supplier check is not a money check. It is a qualitative, flag-based check on the supplier's performance rating (scorecard standing). - **How it is triggered:** The credit limit is calculated from ledger and order totals each time. The supplier check depends on a manually enabled flag (`prevent_pos` or `warn_pos`) and on a scorecard status. The scorecard status comes from periodic scoring criteria such as quality and delivery. - **How it can be overridden:** A credit limit can be bypassed by a role (the credit controller) or by a "bypass credit limit check at Sales Order" setting. The supplier check has no amount to exceed. It is a block or a warning that depends on the supplier's standing. Separately, Purchase Order validation checks things such as minimum order quantity and the `maintain_same_rate` setting. Neither is a supplier spend limit.

final answer
**No. Purchase Order has no automatic spend-limit check against the supplier.** Nothing compares the supplier's outstanding or committed amount to a monetary cap. The only supplier-side gates are status-based, not amount-based. I didn't search for a budget check on Purchase Order, so budget validation is a possible extra gate I haven't verified. **Sales Order's credit limit, for comparison** - `SalesOrder.on_submit` calls `SalesOrder.check_credit_limit`, which calls `check_credit_limit(customer, company)` in `selling/doctype/customer/customer.py`. - `get_credit_limit` looks up the limit in the Customer's `Customer Credit Limit` rows, then the Customer Group's, then the Company's `credit_limit`. - `get_customer_outstanding` computes the customer's current exposure. - If outstanding exceeds the limit, it raises an exception. Users holding the Accounts Settings `credit_controller` role are not blocked. - A `bypass_credit_limit_check` flag can skip the check. - A pre-submit warning, `_check_credit_limit_warn` in `accounts/utils.py`, also runs. **What gates Purchase Order submission instead** 1. **Supplier blocking:** `AccountsController.ensure_supplier_is_not_blocked` in `controllers/accounts_controller.py` is called from `AccountsController.validate`. - For a Purchase Order or Purchase Invoice, it throws "{supplier} is blocked so this transaction cannot proceed" if `Supplier.on_hold` is set. - It only applies when `hold_type` is "All" or "Invoices". - It stops applying once `release_date` has passed. - This is a manual hold flag with an optional expiry. 2. **Supplier Scorecard standing:** `PurchaseOrder.validate_supplier` in `buying/doctype/purchase_order/purchase_order.py` is called from `PurchaseOrder.validate`. - If the Supplier's `prevent_pos` flag is set and a scorecard status exists, it throws "Purchase Orders are not allowed for {0} due to a scorecard standing of {1}". - If `warn_pos` is set, it only shows an orange "Caution" `msgprint`. - The flags come from the Supplier Scorecard Standing fields `prevent_pos` and `warn_pos`. - The scorecard lookup is `frappe.db.get_value("Supplier Scorecard", self.supplier, "status")`, so it only works if the scorecard's name equals the supplier's name. **How these differ from a credit limit** - **Qualitative vs. quantitative:** A credit limit is a numeric, financial-exposure test: outstanding receivables plus open orders against a configured cap. The Purchase Order gates are boolean or status tests on the supplier's standing, with no amounts involved. - **Source of the decision:** The credit limit is computed from ledger and order data, with fallback from customer to customer group to company. The Purchase Order gates come from a manual hold or a performance-scorecard grade. - **When it runs:** Both run during validate or submit. The credit limit also runs on submit and on update after submit, while the Purchase Order checks run in `validate`, so they apply on save as well. - **Override model:** The credit limit has a role-based override (`credit_controller`) and a per-customer bypass flag. Supplier holds and `prevent_pos` have no role override. They are cleared by editing the supplier's hold, its release date, or the scorecard. - **Direction of risk:** A credit limit protects the company from extending too much credit to a customer. The Purchase Order gates protect against buying from an unreliable or blocked vendor.

final answer
**No. Purchase Order has no automatic spend-limit or credit-limit check against the supplier.** The graph search and a grep of `erpnext/buying` found no credit-limit logic for suppliers. `Supplier` has no credit-limit field or `check_credit_limit` equivalent. The credit-limit machinery sits on the selling side: `check_credit_limit()`, `get_credit_limit()` and `get_customer_outstanding()` in `selling/doctype/customer/customer.py`, plus `_check_credit_limit_warn` in `accounts/utils.py`. I did not trace where `validate_supplier` is called from, but a validation hook runs on both save and submit. **What does gate Purchase Order submission** is a supplier scorecard standing check. It is `PurchaseOrder.validate_supplier()` in `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/purchase_order.py` (about lines 272–294). - If `Supplier.prevent_pos` is set, it calls `frappe.throw("Purchase Orders are not allowed for {supplier} due to a scorecard standing of {status}")`. This is a hard block. - If `Supplier.warn_pos` is set, it only shows `frappe.msgprint` with an orange "Caution" message. This is a soft warning. **Where the flags come from:** - `SupplierScorecard.update_standing()` in `buying/doctype/supplier_scorecard/supplier_scorecard.py` sets them (about line 144). - It copies `prevent_pos`, `warn_pos`, `prevent_rfqs` and `warn_rfqs` from the matching standing row onto the Supplier with `frappe.db.set_value`. - The standing row is picked from the scorecard's computed `supplier_score`. - The default standings are Very Poor (prevent), Poor, Average and Excellent. - RFQs have a parallel check on `prevent_rfqs` in `request_for_quotation.py`. **Related, but not a spend gate:** `Supplier.on_hold` with `hold_type` (All, Invoices or Payments) blocks invoices and payments, not PO submission. A Purchase Order's own hold or closed status is checked separately, through `check_for_on_hold_or_closed_status` on linked Material Requests. **How it differs from a credit limit:** - **Quantitative vs qualitative.** A customer credit limit is a monetary check. It compares the customer's outstanding receivables plus the new order against a configured amount, per company, with a bypass option. The supplier check never looks at an amount. It tests a boolean flag. - **Source of the rule.** The credit limit is a financial exposure number set by hand. The PO block is derived from supplier performance, meaning scorecard criteria such as delivery and quality. - **Who it protects.** The credit limit protects the company from credit risk on money owed to it. The scorecard check protects procurement quality and risk, and has nothing to do with how much the company owes the supplier. - **State-based, not computed at submit time.** The flags are cached on the Supplier by the scorecard and read at validation time. Nothing sums open POs or payables.

final answer
No. Purchase Order has no automatic spend-limit check against the supplier. I found no counterpart to `check_credit_limit`, and Supplier has no per-company credit limit or outstanding-balance check that runs on Purchase Order. I read the Purchase Order's `validate` and `on_submit` methods in `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/purchase_order.py` directly. The Sales Order side comes from the codegraph results. **The Sales Order check** - `SalesOrder.check_credit_limit` (`sales_order.py:514`) calls `erpnext.selling.doctype.customer.customer.check_credit_limit(customer, company)`. - It reads the `Customer Credit Limit` child table, which holds a limit per company, and honours a `bypass_credit_limit_check` flag. - It compares the customer's outstanding balance and open orders against that limit, so it caps how much the party can owe you. - Sales Invoice, Delivery Note and Journal Entry call the same function. **What gates Purchase Order submission** 1. **Supplier standing flags, checked in `validate`.** `PurchaseOrder.validate_supplier` (line 272) reads `Supplier.prevent_pos` and `Supplier.warn_pos`. - If `prevent_pos` is set and a `Supplier Scorecard` standing exists, it throws and blocks the Purchase Order. - If `warn_pos` is set, it only shows an orange "Caution" message. - The flags and the scorecard standing are set by the Supplier Scorecard evaluation, not by the order amount. Nothing sums money against a limit. 2. **Budget validation, run in `on_submit`.** `self.validate_budget()` (line 386) checks the spend against the `Budget` doctype. That budget is set per cost center, account or project and fiscal period. It is a limit on your own spending, not a limit on the supplier. 3. **Approval rules, also in `on_submit`.** `Authorization Control.validate_approving_authority(doctype, company, base_grand_total)` (line 388) checks the order total against authorization rules. They can restrict by user, role or amount, so a user or role without authority cannot submit above a threshold. 4. **Other checks.** - `check_for_on_hold_or_closed_status("Material Request", ...)` blocks a Purchase Order against a linked Material Request that is Closed or On Hold. It is a status check, not a money check. - `validate_minimum_order_qty`, `validate_for_items` and `validate_against_blanket_order` are item and quantity checks. **How this differs from a credit limit** - A credit limit is a per-party, per-company financial exposure cap. It is based on the counterparty's outstanding balance and uncleared orders, and it can be bypassed per customer. - The Purchase Order gates are different in nature: - Supplier flags are qualitative: scorecard standing, with a hard block or a warning. - Budget is internal: it limits your own spending by cost center or account. - Authorization rules are about who may approve and at what amount. - None of them says "you may not owe this supplier more than X". If you want a supplier credit cap, you would have to build it as a custom check.

final answer
Nothing in this codebase does that cascade, as far as I can find. I've searched for it and found the opposite behavior. **What the code does.** `PurchaseReceipt.on_cancel` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` (around line 437) doesn't cancel the invoice. It first calls `super().on_cancel()` and checks the on-hold/closed status of the linked Purchase Order. Then it looks for a submitted invoice: ```python submitted = frappe.get_all( "Purchase Invoice Item", filters={"purchase_receipt": self.name, "docstatus": 1}, fields=["parent"], as_list=True, limit=1, ) if submitted: frappe.throw(_("Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted").format(submitted[0][0])) ``` **How it finds the invoice.** It queries the `Purchase Invoice Item` child table for rows where `purchase_receipt` equals the receipt's name and `docstatus` is 1. The `parent` of the first match is the invoice name. `Purchase Invoice Item.purchase_receipt` is the link field. **Result.** If a submitted Purchase Invoice is linked, cancelling the Purchase Receipt is blocked with an error. You have to cancel the invoice first. The user has to do that by hand. **What I searched.** I looked for `.cancel()` calls and `cancel_*` or `delete_*` helpers in non-test code under `erpnext/`. None of the matches cancels a Purchase Invoice from a Purchase Receipt. The closest are unrelated: `cancel_linked_invoices` in `pos_invoice_merge_log.py` (POS/Sales Invoice) and the Unreconcile Payment cleanup in `accounts_controller.py`. I didn't check Frappe's own linked-document handling (`frappe.desk.form.linked_with`), which lives outside this repo. Any automatic cancellation would have to come from there.

final answer
In this ERPNext codebase, no method cascade-cancels a linked Purchase Invoice when you cancel a Purchase Receipt. The premise of the question is wrong, because the code blocks the cancellation instead. **What happens.** `PurchaseReceipt.on_cancel` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` (around line 437) calls `super().on_cancel()` and `check_for_on_hold_or_closed_status`. It then looks for a submitted invoice with this query: ```python frappe.get_all("Purchase Invoice Item", filters={"purchase_receipt": self.name, "docstatus": 1}, fields=["parent"], as_list=True, limit=1) ``` If that returns a row, it calls `frappe.throw(_("Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted").format(...))`. You have to cancel the invoice yourself first. **How it finds the invoice.** It queries the child table `Purchase Invoice Item`. It matches rows whose `purchase_receipt` field equals the receipt's name and whose `docstatus` is 1. The invoice name is the row's `parent`. The check on the submit path (around line 435) has the same "already submitted" message. **What I checked.** I searched the code for any `.cancel()` call or cancel-cascade helper that touches a Purchase Invoice from a Purchase Receipt, and found none. The only cascade-style helper I found is `cancel_linked_invoices` in `pos_invoice_merge_log.py`, which is about POS invoices and is unrelated. My `.cancel()` search was capped at 80 results, so it wasn't exhaustive. **What does run on cancel.** After the check, `on_cancel` calls `update_prevdoc_status`, `update_billing_status`, `update_stock_ledger`, `make_gl_entries_on_cancel` and `repost_future_sle_and_gle`. The `BuyingController.on_cancel` parent method in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py` also runs, and it updates the last purchase rate and removes any linked assets.

final answer
The premise doesn't hold: ERPNext has no method that cancels a linked Purchase Invoice when you cancel a Purchase Receipt. It blocks the cancellation instead. The check is in `PurchaseReceipt.on_cancel()` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` (around lines 437–450). It runs `frappe.get_all("Purchase Invoice Item", filters={"purchase_receipt": self.name, "docstatus": 1}, fields=["parent"], limit=1)`. If that finds a submitted invoice item pointing at the receipt, it calls `frappe.throw(_("Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted").format(...))`. So the user has to cancel the Purchase Invoice first. This is how it finds the invoice: it queries the child table `Purchase Invoice Item`, whose `purchase_receipt` field links back to the receipt, and takes the invoice name from the `parent` column. Only after that check passes does `on_cancel()` go on to update the billing status, reverse the stock ledger and GL entries, and so on. I looked for a cascade elsewhere and found none. The searches covered `.cancel()` calls across `erpnext/`, which turned up nothing that touches Purchase Invoices. They also covered `PurchaseReceipt` and `BuyingController` for any cancel-linked-invoice logic.

final answer
The block is `SellingController.validate_selling_price()` in `erpnext/controllers/selling_controller.py` (around lines 295–349). Sales Invoice and Delivery Note both inherit from `SellingController`. I read the code but didn't run it. **When it runs:** `SellingController.validate()` calls it at line 65. That happens when the document is validated, which includes submission. It throws `frappe.throw(..., title="Invalid Selling Price")` through the inner `throw_message()` helper, so the save or submit fails. **What it checks, for each item row:** 1. It skips the whole check if the document is a return (`is_return`) or if the **Selling Settings → "Validate Selling Price"** checkbox (`validate_selling_price`) is off. That checkbox is the bypass the error message points to. 2. It skips free items and rows with no item code. 3. **Last purchase rate:** if `base_net_rate` is below the item's `last_purchase_rate × conversion_factor`, it throws with "last purchase rate". 4. **Valuation rate:** this check is skipped for internal customers and for non-stock items. Otherwise it compares `base_net_rate` with `rate_field × conversion_factor`: - On Sales Invoice and Delivery Note, `rate_field` is the row's `incoming_rate`, which is the actual stock valuation at the time of sale. - On Sales Order and Quotation, `rate_field` is `valuation_rate`. - If the net rate is lower, it throws with "valuation rate". The error reads: "Row #N: Selling rate for item X is lower than its valuation rate (or last purchase rate). Selling net rate should be at least Y." The same method is also called from `erpnext/accounts/services/child_item_update.py` (line 174). A related test is `test_validate_selling_price` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/test_delivery_note.py`.

Selling net rate should be at least Y." - The message also tells the user they can disable the setting in Selling Settings to bypass the check. `validate_selling_price` is also called from `ChildItemUpdater._post_update` in `erpnext/accounts/services/child_item_update.py`.

final answer
The check is `SellingController.validate_selling_price()` in `erpnext/controllers/selling_controller.py`, at about line 295. I read the code but didn't run it. **How it's triggered** - `SellingController.validate()` calls it on every save and submit. Sales Invoice, Delivery Note, Sales Order and Quotation all inherit from `SellingController`, so they all run it. - `erpnext/accounts/services/child_item_update.py` also calls it from `_post_update`, so a Sales Order edited through "Update Items" after submit gets the same check. **When it is skipped** - The document is a return (`is_return`). - The "Validate Selling Price" setting (`validate_selling_price`) in Selling Settings is switched off. The error message names this setting as the way to bypass the check. - The item has no `item_code`, or it is a free item (`is_free_item`). **What it checks, per item row** 1. **Last purchase rate.** It reads `last_purchase_rate` from the Item and multiplies it by the row's `conversion_factor`. If `base_net_rate` is below that, it throws. 2. **Valuation cost.** This is skipped for internal customers and for non-stock items. - For Sales Invoice and Delivery Note, the reference field is the row's `incoming_rate`, which is the stock valuation cost at the time of sale. - For Sales Order and Quotation, it uses the row's `valuation_rate` instead. - If the row has a value in that field and `base_net_rate` is below it (scaled by `conversion_factor`), it throws. **How it blocks** - Both checks go through the inner helper `throw_message()`. It calls `frappe.throw` with the title "Invalid Selling Price". - The message reads "Row #X: Selling rate for item … is lower than its valuation rate (or last purchase rate). Selling net rate should be at least …". - Because this runs in `validate`, the document can't be saved or submitted until the rate is fixed or the setting is disabled.
